# Quadrotor Test Registry

Purpose: quick duplicate check before spending GPU time. Keep this file short.
Use `EXPERIMENTS.md` for the detailed lab notebook and older history.

Current next ID: `QDR-355`.

## Rule

Before launching a training, eval, sweep, or diagnostic run that we may cite:

1. Search this file for the scenario, changed knob, seed, and budget.
2. If the question is already answered, do not rerun it.
3. If it is new, reserve the next `QDR-###` ID first.
4. Change one main variable unless the test is explicitly a robustness gate.
5. Pass/fail uses deterministic mean-action eval, not the sampled training HUD.

## Current Promoted Benchmark

Status: regression harness, not the main research direction.

As of May 19, 2026, do not spend more GPU time squeezing this waypoint-assisted
velocity-control benchmark unless the change protects the new privileged-learning
track or catches a regression. The main direction is documented in `ROADMAP.md`:
Swift-style privileged training, deployable onboard observations, and
thrust/body-rate control.

As of May 20, 2026, track fast indoor flight and indoor exploration separately.
Fast flight may use known route/goal information and is judged by speed,
success, clearance, crashes, and dynamics stress. Exploration must not rely on
hidden target/waypoint breadcrumbs in the actor and is judged by coverage over
time, crash rate, revisits, target discovery, and time-to-detect. Do not compare
route success directly against exploration success; reserve separate QDR IDs for
each track.

Scenario: `route_layout_mixed_waypoint_level`.

This is a fixed-altitude, waypoint-assisted indoor navigation benchmark with a
simple fixed-layout sampler. Each reset chooses one of:

- center block
- doorway
- corridor
- passable maze

It is still not pure exploration and still uses waypoint/path observations. It
is now a stronger baby-step than the single passable-maze benchmark because it
tests geometry transfer without adding vision-model complexity.

Promoted training command:

```bash
QUAD_GPU_SYNC=0 QUAD_CHECKPOINT_INTERVAL=5 QUAD_TAG=layout_mixed_path035_kiz4_std020_from_zero_50m_s<seed> scripts/quadrotor_remote_gpu.sh train route_layout_mixed_waypoint_level 50000000 <seed> none
```

Promoted eval commands:

```bash
QUAD_GPU_SYNC=0 scripts/quadrotor_remote_gpu.sh eval-checkpoints route_layout_mixed_waypoint_level 4 512 experiments/puffer_quadrotor_177911247235

QUAD_GPU_SYNC=0 QUAD_GAUNTLET_OBS_PROFILE=layout_mixed_path035_std020 scripts/quadrotor_remote_gpu.sh route-gauntlet experiments/puffer_quadrotor_177911247235/model_puffer_quadrotor_000155.pt frontier
```

Visual command:

```bash
scripts/quadrotor_visual_eval.sh route_layout_mixed_waypoint_level
```

Promoted checkpoint:
`experiments/puffer_quadrotor_177911247235/model_puffer_quadrotor_000155.pt`.

Current result: the 50M recipe passed a 3-seed from-zero gate. Best deterministic
mixed-layout eval results:

- seed 91: `99.46%`, checkpoint `experiments/puffer_quadrotor_177911247235/model_puffer_quadrotor_000155.pt`
- seed 88: `99.27%`, checkpoint `experiments/puffer_quadrotor_177911308822/model_puffer_quadrotor_000170.pt`
- seed 90: `99.37%`, checkpoint `experiments/puffer_quadrotor_177911345652/model_puffer_quadrotor_000160.pt`

Frontier gauntlet means are `99.26%`/`99.02%`/`98.93%` for seeds 91/88/90.
The weakest frontier case is still sequential routing: `goals_3` at
`96.78%`/`95.21%`/`96.20%`.

## Recent Evidence

| ID | Name | Question | Result | Lesson |

| QDR-354 | `native_ppo_depth_camera_compare` | User correctly challenged QDR-353: why are we not using PufferLib PPO when iteration speed is the whole point? Test the depth camera in native PPO first. Controlled variants: `baseline`, `cam16`, `cam20`; first run should use `baseline cam20` on `maze` for a fast direct-RL signal. Runner: `scripts/quadrotor4_qdr354_native_ppo_camera.sh [baseline|cam16|cam20|all]`. | Failed as a learning result, passed as a harness correction. Added native PPO runner plus camera-aware native eval flags in `scripts/quadrotor4_eval_headless.py` and `scripts/quadrotor4_eval_checkpoints.py`. Local checks and remote smoke passed, then `beeblebrox` ran `PYTHON=.venv/bin/python scripts/quadrotor4_qdr354_native_ppo_camera.sh baseline cam20` on `maze`. Throughput was about `450K` SPS for baseline and `190K` SPS for `cam20`; the native PPO loop works, but env stepping dominates and the camera raycasts slow it down. Last-3 deterministic checkpoint evals over 4096 episodes: baseline best `success=0`, `coverage=0.455604`, `obstacle_hit=0.001953`, `timeout=0.998047`, `score=-18.1969`; `cam20` best `success=0`, `coverage=0.449609`, `obstacle_hit=0.004639`, `timeout=0.995361`, `score=-31.9458`. Logs: `logs/qdr354/maze_baseline/eval_last3_s88.log` and `logs/qdr354/maze_cam20/eval_last3_s88.log` on the GPU machine. | Native PPO is the right default harness for fast iteration. The negative result is also useful: simply appending dense depth-camera pixels to the current direct exploration PPO task does not make the policy use vision. Future camera work should use a diagnostic auxiliary task or architecture that forces local geometry use, and should keep the fastest native PPO loop unless privileged labels are specifically being tested. |
| QDR-353 | `depth_camera_target_distill_compare` | Does the validated deployable depth-camera observation help the recurrent target-distill actor learn the strict p3/procedural-house privileged-unexplored target task, compared against the same setup with no camera? Controlled variants: `baseline`, `cam16`, and `cam20` using the same seed, teacher, train modes, safety layer, rollout schedule, and deployable coverage input. Runner: `scripts/quadrotor4_qdr353_depth_camera_compare.sh [baseline|cam16|cam20|all]`. | Stopped, not promoted. Local and remote smokes passed, proving the script and camera checkpoint metadata work. A full remote run started on `beeblebrox`, but it was CPU-bound: about `2.3GB` GPU memory, `1-2%` GPU util, and `850-870%` Python CPU. Baseline training reached useful rollout stats by update 120 (`success=0.9767`, `coverage=0.9808`, `obstacle=0.0063`, `timeout=0.0170`) but runtime was dominated by Python/CPU rollout and label generation. The run was killed before completion after user challenged the method choice. | Correct lesson: this was the wrong first camera-learning harness. Target distillation can answer a later supervised-learning question, but it throws away PufferLib PPO throughput. Move to QDR-354 native PPO for fast iteration. |
| QDR-352 | `depth_camera_resolution_headroom_latest` | After QDR-351 proved the fixed camera, can we port the quadrotor worktree onto latest upstream PufferLib `origin/4.0` (`9836f0d2`) and make the depth camera less coarse without changing the fixed `416`-float quadrotor ABI or breaking the default 16x12 probe? | Passed as infrastructure, not a learning result. Created latest-upstream worktree branch `codex/quadrotor-4-latest`, ported the quadrotor changes, and rebuilt on the newer PufferLib base. Replaced the hard-coded camera size with bounded runtime args: default `16x12`, max/reserved `20x15`, stride `20`, written into the existing exploration patch tail `obs[60:360]` when `camera_depth_obs` is enabled. Added env keys and CLI flags `camera_depth_width` / `camera_depth_height` and `--camera-depth-width` / `--camera-depth-height`. Checks passed: `../../.venv/bin/python -m py_compile scripts/quadrotor4_depth_camera_probe.py scripts/quadrotor4_frontier_target_distill.py`; `bash -n scripts/quadrotor4.sh`; `PYTHON=../../.venv/bin/python scripts/quadrotor4.sh build-cpu`; default probe `KMP_DUPLICATE_LIB_OK=TRUE PYTHON=../../.venv/bin/python scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-depth-camera-probe --compare-disabled --print-agents 1 --total-agents 8 --modes doorway maze`; high-res probe with `--camera-depth-width 20 --camera-depth-height 15 --compare-disabled --print-agents 1 --total-agents 8 --modes doorway maze procedural_house_multizone_p3`. Default `16x12` stayed `obs_size=416`: doorway `min=0.2206 max=1.0000 mean=0.6056 center=0.6596 front_lidar=0.6579`, maze `min=0.1347 max=0.8845 mean=0.3902 center=0.3579 front_lidar=0.3568`. High-res `20x15` also stayed `obs_size=416`: doorway `mean=0.6067 center=0.6586`, maze `mean=0.3928 center=0.3572`, p3 house `min=0.1595 max=0.7906 mean=0.3319 center=0.2648 front_lidar=0.2645`. | Use QDR-352 as the camera plumbing baseline on latest PufferLib. Do not claim learning progress from it. The next useful rung is a controlled training comparison: depth-camera deployable actor at `16x12` versus `20x15` on a small local-clearance/obstacle-avoidance or p3 target-distillation task, then only scale to exploration if the learned policy actually uses the image-like signal. |
| QDR-351 | `depth_camera_sensor_validation` | Before trying pixel/depth-camera learning, can we add an opt-in egocentric depth camera and prove the observation is geometrically sane without changing the fixed `416`-float quadrotor ABI or breaking deployable masking? | Passed as a sensor/infrastructure validation, not a learning result. Added a fixed `16x12` forward depth camera using native C raycasts against room planes, floor/ceiling, and obstacle AABBs. The camera is opt-in via `camera_depth_obs` and writes into the front of the existing exploration patch tail (`obs[60:252]`), so old checkpoints remain compatible when the flag is off. Added `--camera-depth-obs`, FOV args, `--deploy-camera-depth-obs`, and `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-depth-camera-probe`. Local checks: `../../.venv/bin/python -m py_compile scripts/quadrotor4_depth_camera_probe.py scripts/quadrotor4_frontier_target_distill.py`; `PYTHON=../../.venv/bin/python scripts/quadrotor4.sh build-cpu`; `KMP_DUPLICATE_LIB_OK=TRUE PYTHON=../../.venv/bin/python scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-depth-camera-probe --compare-disabled --print-agents 1 --total-agents 16 --modes doorway maze procedural_house_multizone_p3`. Probe results were bounded and nonconstant: doorway `min=0.1952 max=1.0000 mean=0.6040`, maze `min=0.1347 max=1.0000 mean=0.4096`, p3 house `min=0.1395 max=0.9210 mean=0.3193`; disabled-patch mean absolute diffs were `0.6009/0.4115/0.3232`. Center-depth and old front-lidar agree in the focused rerun: doorway `0.6596` vs `0.6579`, maze `0.3579` vs `0.3568`. Deployable masking check preserved camera values only when `deploy_camera_depth_obs=True`. | The camera signal itself works and matches the old body-frame ray convention. Do not train from it yet as if this proves pixels/depth solve exploration. Next step should be a small, controlled learning/probe rung: either clone/predict local clearance or compare depth-camera deployable actor inputs against the existing ray/patch baseline on a simplified obstacle-avoidance or p3 target-distillation task. |
| QDR-350 | `explicit_planner_candidate_cache` | QDR-348/QDR-349 both point at the same structural bug: graph candidates are currently an observation-tail side effect, not a stable planner output. Build the candidate cache into the C planner, then test whether candidate-index control is a valid next abstraction before spending GPU on longer training. | Mixed engineering pass, method fail. Implemented a first-class C cache for top-4 graph candidates, plus a candidate-head runner `scripts/quadrotor4_qdr350_candidate_cache_clone.sh`. Local checks passed: Python compile, CPU backend build, and 2-update candidate-head smoke. The initial selected-label smoke fixed the immediate interface (`graph_valid=1.0000` locally) but the first GPU selected-clone run on `beeblebrox` exposed rollout decay (`graph_valid=0.0199` at u20), so it was stopped. Diagnosis showed the selected flag goes stale when the graph selector hands off to region continuation. Adding region IDs improved this but did not make selected-clone a meaningful training target. The stronger privileged-candidate diagnostic then fixed candidate availability but exposed the real abstraction flaw: candidate labels need global frontier identity while actions need local path-step directions. Splitting candidate features into path-step direction plus final-frontier bearing made the interface more coherent, but the candidate-projected privileged oracle is still bad on p3: local 512-env probe at horizon 1200 got `0%` success, `16.58%` coverage, `100%` timeout, `0%` contacts, while the onboard graph path oracle under the same `0.99` coverage gate reached `1.17%` success and `71.92%` coverage. | Do not train the current top-4 candidate-index head longer. The cache/interface bug is real and partly fixed, but the method still loses essential topology: projecting privileged nearest-unexplored onto four local graph candidates produces a safe but nearly stationary explorer. The next step is not PPO tuning. Either build a richer planner/actor interface with explicit topological/path state and candidate commitment, or train against planner outputs that already encode path progress rather than asking one discrete candidate index to recover full exploration behavior. |
| QDR-349 | `fast_house_graph_selected_candidate_clone` | QDR-348 showed that projecting privileged unexplored labels onto the top-4 graph candidates creates a bad teacher. Sanity question: can the discrete candidate head at least clone the deployable graph selector's own selected candidate when labels come from the candidate `selected` flag under `explore_frontier_target_mode=onboard`? This is not expected to beat QDR-346; it isolates candidate-head/train-loop validity from privileged-label mismatch. Command: `scripts/quadrotor4_qdr349_selected_candidate_clone.sh`. | Failed as a candidate-head validation because the selected-candidate label source is not stable. GPU on `beeblebrox`: stopped at u40 after low valid-label rate and obstacle-heavy teacher rollout. Train stream: u20 `graph_loss=1.1087`, `graph_valid=0.1966`, success `0%`, obstacle `100%`; u40 `graph_loss=0.9555`, `graph_valid=0.1475`, success `0%`, obstacle `100%`; u40 checkpoint saved. Deterministic p3 u40 eval: `0%` success, `33.78%` coverage, `98.68%` timeout, `1.32%` obstacle, `frontier_graph_active_rate=0.147`. Direct selected-candidate oracle eval: `0%` success, `30.62%` coverage, `98.83%` timeout, `1.17%` obstacle, `frontier_graph_active_rate=0.210`. Artifacts: `logs/qdr349_remote/train_s88.log`, `experiments/qdr349/checkpoints/qdr349_selected_candidate_clone_s88_u000040.pt`. | Do not train selected-flag candidate clones until the env exposes candidates as first-class planner state. The current `selected` feature is often absent because the observation-tail recomputation and the onboard graph planner's selected target are not the same stable object. This is an implementation/interface blocker, not evidence that discrete candidate selection is hopeless. |
| QDR-348 | `fast_house_graph_candidate_selector` | QDR-347 showed passive graph-candidate features do not fix privileged target-vector imitation: deterministic student timeouts still chose bad geometry despite `pred_exists=1`. Hypothesis: make the top-4 deployable graph candidates the action abstraction itself. Train a discrete candidate-selection head from privileged unexplored labels, then convert the selected candidate into the existing velocity-target controller. This keeps actor inputs deployable while using privileged map truth only for labels. Command: `scripts/quadrotor4_qdr348_candidate_selector.sh`. | Failed as a solution, passed as a stronger diagnosis. Local checks passed: Python compile, shell syntax, CPU build, 2-update CPU train smoke, and 4-episode deterministic eval smoke. GPU run on `beeblebrox` wrote u50 and was stopped early after bad signals. Train stream: u20 `graph_loss=1.378`, `graph_valid=0.450`, success `0%`, obstacle `100%`; u40 `graph_loss=1.380`, `graph_valid=0.367`, success `40%`, obstacle `60%`; u50 checkpoint saved; u60 before stop `graph_valid=0.330`, student_prob `0.030`. Deterministic p3 u50 eval: `0%` success, `32.56%` coverage, `99.37%` timeout, `0.63%` obstacle. The projected candidate-label teacher itself was weak: p3 oracle under `--predict-graph-candidate` got only `0.78%` success, `39.99%` coverage, `98.93%` timeout, `0.29%` obstacle. Artifacts: `logs/qdr348_remote/train_s88.log`, `experiments/qdr348/checkpoints/qdr348_graph_candidate_selector_s88_u000050.pt`. | Do not train this privileged-projected candidate selector longer. The bottleneck is not just model capacity; the top-4 graph candidate set plus dot-product privileged projection does not contain/choose a good approximation of the privileged unexplored teacher. Next isolate whether the discrete candidate head can clone a valid deployable selected candidate at all, then build a better FUEL-lite/topological candidate generator or candidate-label teacher rather than optimizing this projection. |
| QDR-347 | `fast_house_graph_candidate_privileged_distill` | QDR-346 proves a deployable graph selector helps, but the learned actor still only gets a single target vector. Hypothesis: exposing the top graph frontier candidates as compact deployable observation features gives MinGRU enough spatial choice state to imitate the privileged unexplored teacher without privileged actor leakage. Implementation reserves the final 32 floats of the existing exploration patch tail for four graph candidates: direction, depth, unknown-component gain, cluster size, passage quality, selected flag, and exists flag. Command: `scripts/quadrotor4_qdr347_graph_candidate_distill.sh` from zero by default; set `QDR347_DEPLOY_PATCH=1` only for the patch-plus-candidate ablation. | Failed as a solution, passed as diagnosis. Local checks passed: Python compile, CPU build, no-render oracle smoke, and direct obs probe confirmed `obs=(4,416)` with nonzero graph-candidate tail while `deployable_input` zeroes the rest of the patch when `--deploy-graph-candidate-obs` is used alone. GPU run on `beeblebrox` was stopped at update `150` after deterministic evals failed. Training stream looked good because teacher/mixed rollout still dominated: u150 `student_prob=0.143`, success `98.75%`, coverage `98.80%`, obstacle `0.19%`, timeout `1.05%`. Deterministic p3 eval was bad: u100 `0%` success, `26.50%` coverage, `100%` timeout; u150 `0%` success, `30.57%` coverage, `99.80%` timeout, `0.20%` obstacle. Diagnostic at u150: student `0%` success / `99.80%` timeout while oracle on the same profile gets `98.25%` success; timeout states have `pred_exists=1`, `oracle_exists=1`, but `pred_oracle_angle=116.0deg`, `pred_target_ray=0.58m` versus `oracle_target_ray=3.54m`, and `action_speed=0.055`. Artifacts: `logs/qdr347_remote/train_s88.log`, `experiments/qdr347/checkpoints/qdr347_graph_candidates_priv_unexplored_s88_u000{050,100,150}.pt`. | Do not repeat bare privileged target-vector imitation by just adding candidate features as passive inputs. The failure changed from target-existence collapse to wrong target selection/clearance: the policy sees a target should exist but chooses directions that are geometrically bad. Next higher-leverage move is to make the graph candidates part of the supervised decision itself: discrete candidate-index / candidate scoring head, auxiliary labels for which candidate matches privileged unexplored progress, or RL over candidate selection with privileged critic/reward. Also cache candidate computation before scaling this because recomputing graph candidates in every observation made rollout much slower. |

| QDR-345 | `fast_house_frontier_graph_ablation` | QDR-344 improved p3 with a deployable frontier graph, but the gain might be incidental frontier rescoring. Logs: `logs/qdr345_remote/`. | Unknown-component spatial value is the real signal; removing it collapses below the old onboard baseline. | QDR-344 | `fast_house_frontier_graph_probe` | QDR-342/QDR-343 rejected bare privileged target-vector imitation, and QDR-341 rejected traversing unknown space as free. Next step should either optimize/ablate the graph selector against QDR-340 or build a richer deployable spatial-memory policy interface; more bare target-vector DAgger is still not justified. |
| QDR-343 | `fast_house_privileged_unexplored_onpolicy_dagger` | QDR-342's teacher-assisted training stream looked strong, but every deterministic checkpoint collapsed. Logs: `logs/qdr343_remote/`. | Aggressive on-policy DAgger improves coverage only marginally and adds contacts; it does not close the privileged-teacher gap. The blocker is not just DAgger schedule. A target-vector policy with local rays/patch/coverage lacks enough persistent spatial state/action grounding to reproduce privileged unexplored search. | QDR-342 | `fast_house_privileged_unexplored_distill` | QDR-340 proved privileged nearest-unexplored target selection nearly solves p3, while QDR-341 proved naive deployable optimistic traversal is much worse than baseline. Runner: `scripts/quadrotor4_qdr342_privileged_unexplored_distill.sh`. GPU from-zero, 250 updates, teacher rollout updates `60`, max student rollout prob `0.45`. Training HUD remained high under mixed teacher/student control, but deterministic eval failed: final p3 `0%` success / `28.56%` coverage / `0.83%` contacts; aggregate `3.50%` / `26.43%` / `0.43%`. Earlier checkpoints u50/u100/u150/u200 were all `0%` p3 success with `20.06%`/`23.94%`/`16.68%`/`28.96%` coverage. Logs: `logs/qdr342_remote/`. | Privileged unexplored labels alone are not enough under mostly teacher-assisted rollout. The model does not learn a closed-loop search policy from local deployable observations; teacher actions mask compounding error. Next test should be aggressive on-policy DAgger or a richer memory/map architecture, not more updates with the same schedule. |
| QDR-341 | `fast_house_onboard_optimistic_unknown` | QDR-340 showed privileged `unexplored` nearly solves p3 at the 1200-step budget. Its key advantage is not fancy scoring: it plans through true valid but currently unexplored cells to the nearest unseen cell. Can a deployable optimistic onboard planner approximate that by treating unknown, not-yet-known-blocked cells as tentatively traversable, relying on lidar/safety/stale suppression to reject blocked unknowns? | Failed decisively. Added `--explore-onboard-optimistic-unknown` and runner `scripts/quadrotor4_qdr341_optimistic_unknown_probe.sh`. GPU forced p3 speed `0.72`, 2048 episodes: horizon `1200` oracle `0%` success / `26.95%` coverage / `0%` contacts, QDR-316 `0%` / `27.31%` / `0.049%`; horizon `1600` oracle `0%` / `27.32%` / `0%`, QDR-316 `0%` / `27.64%` / `0.146%`. Logs: `logs/qdr341_remote/`. | Do not use naive unknown-space traversal. It walks long paths but collapses new-cell rate and keeps target cluster size near one, so it is choosing low-value adjacent unknowns or impossible unknown corridors. The right next step is privileged-label learning or a constrained visibility/topology planner, not pretending all unknown cells are traversable. |
| QDR-340 | `fast_house_privileged_region_ceiling` | QDR-339 showed deployable cluster-rate scoring makes p3 worse, not better. Is the 1200-step house-p3 plateau mainly an onboard-planner limitation or a task-budget/geometry limit? Speed `0.90`, oracle `44.42%` / `28.31%` timeout / `27.27%` contacts, coverage `80.44%`; QDR-316 `41.14%` / `24.42%` timeout / `34.44%` contacts, coverage `77.13%`. Logs are under `logs/qdr320_remote/`. | This is the new honest limit. The current planner/policy can eventually solve large houses, but fast 99% coverage within `1200` steps exposes a speed-safety frontier: speed `0.70` is useful but slightly unsafe; speed `0.90` is clearly unsafe. QDR-316 remains very close to oracle, so the bottleneck is the deployable planner/controller profile, not student target prediction. |
| QDR-319 | `procedural_house_multizone_scale_probe` | QDR-318 showed `8m` multizone houses are mostly solved. Does scaling the same topology up expose a speed/horizon/late-discovery limit before we build a new metric or multi-layer generator? | Built reproducible runner `scripts/quadrotor4_qdr319_scale_probe.sh`. GPU results with QDR-316 u200 and oracle, seed `88`, goal `0.99`, all `0%` contacts/timeouts: room `10m`, `cell=0.35`, `4096` steps: oracle `100%`, QDR-316 `100%`; room `10m`, `cell=0.35`, `3072` steps: oracle `100%`, QDR-316 `100%`; room `12m`, `cell=0.40`, `4096` steps: oracle `100%`, QDR-316 `100%`. Mean episode lengths were about `1172` steps for `10m` and `1318-1327` for `12m`; path length rose to about `197-199m` in `12m`. The runner writes logs to `logs/qdr319/` when rerun; the numeric result is recorded here from the GPU stdout. | Scaling single-floor 2D house coverage is not exposing a learning limit yet. QDR-316 is now oracle-matched on these bigger layouts. The useful next axis is fast-exploration pressure, explicit room/connector discovery metrics, or a genuine 2.5D/multifloor task; do not train on QDR-319 as if it is harder. |
| QDR-318 | `procedural_house_multizone_v0` | QDR-317 proved the first single-floor house generator is useful infrastructure but already solved by QDR-316. First draft was too hard/malformed for the oracle (`~90.8%` aggregate, p0 `~52.9%` locally), so the generator was corrected by widening doors to `0.42-0.56`, removing the malformed p0 central wall, and reducing clutter/snake density. GPU gate, seed `88`, room `8.0m`, `cell_size=0.35`, `4096` max steps, QDR-311/QDR-316 stack, all `0%` contacts: goal `0.95`, oracle `99.1854%`, QDR-316 `99.4501%`; goal `0.98`, oracle `98.9959%`, QDR-316 `99.2289%`; goal `0.99`, oracle `98.3199%`, QDR-316 `99.0316%`. Weakest template at `0.99` is p0: oracle `92.59%`, QDR-316 `94.89%`. No-render visual smoke for QDR-316 at goal `0.99`, `32` episodes: `100%` success, `99.22%` coverage, `0%` contacts/timeouts, `wall_stall_logs=1`. Logs are under `logs/qdr318_remote/`. | Useful harder regression infrastructure, but not a new policy-training target. QDR-316 matches or beats the oracle on aggregate and passes visual smoke cleanly. The next push should change the benchmark axis: scale/horizon pressure, explicit room/connector discovery, room revisit ratio, target/exploration search, or 2.5D/multifloor structure. |
| QDR-317 | `procedural_house_v0` | Can we add a PufferLib-native house-like exploration generator, inspired by HouseExpo/ProcTHOR-style room topology but kept in the fast C box-obstacle backend, and does it expose a harder limit than saturated tight-p1? | Built `procedural_house` plus forced templates `procedural_house_p0..p3` as obstacle modes `23..27`, raised `QR_MAX_OBSTACLES` to `32`, and wired Python mode maps/gauntlet support. GPU eval, room `8.0m`, `cell_size=0.35`, `4096` steps, QDR-311/QDR-316 stack: at goal `0.98`, oracle `99.82%` success / `0.18%` timeout / `0%` contacts; QDR-316 u200 `99.84%` / `0.16%` / `0%`. At goal `0.99`, oracle `99.68%` / `0.32%` / `0%`; QDR-316 u200 `99.74%` / `0.26%` / `0%`. Pattern 2 is the weakest template: oracle `98.87%`, QDR-316 `99.05%` at `0.99`. Logs are under `logs/qdr317_remote/`. | House v0 is useful infrastructure but not a new learning limit. It proves the generator is reachable, fast, rendered by the existing harness, and compatible with deployable observations, but QDR-316 already transfers almost perfectly. Do not train on this as if it will move the research frontier. Next build should be a genuinely harder house benchmark: larger multi-zone houses, floor-to-floor connectors/stairwell/shaft volumes, room discovery metrics, or late-frontier completion pressure beyond `0.99` coverage. |
| QDR-316 | `cell035_tight_distill` | QDR-315 showed `cell_size=0.35` is the right tight-topology runtime/eval scale, and QDR-312 u400 works well under an env override. Can we train/distill with `0.35m` directly so the checkpoint no longer relies on an eval override, while preserving hard/fixed skill and improving tight-p1's remaining `2-3%` timeout tail? | Promoted. First GPU attempt was invalid: init-checkpoint loading silently restored QDR-312's saved `cell_size=0.30`, producing poor `91-92%` tight-p1 results. Added train-time `--ignore-checkpoint-env-config`, reran corrected `scripts/quadrotor4_qdr316_cell035_distill.sh`, and verified saved checkpoints contain `cell_size=0.35`. Best checkpoint: `experiments/qdr316/checkpoints_preserve/qdr316_cell035_preserve_distill_s88_u000200.pt`. Tight-p1 seeds `88/90/91`: `97.96/98.16/97.24%` success, `2.04/1.84/2.76%` timeout, all `0%` contacts. Regression seeds `90/91`: p0 `99.70/99.74%`, p2 `99.80/99.85%`, p3 `99.91/99.93%`, all `0%` contacts. No-render visual tight-p1 seed `88`, `32` episodes: `32/32`, `0%` contacts/timeouts, `wall_stall_logs=5` versus QDR-312+0.35's `88`. Logs are under `logs/qdr316_remote/`; candidate checkpoint fetched locally. | Direct `0.35m` training is now the promoted tight-topology baseline. The gain over QDR-312+override is modest in deterministic success average but meaningful in visual stall behavior. Do not repeat the invalid first QDR-316 run or trust any init-checkpoint experiment unless checkpoint env config is explicitly controlled. Next limit is no longer doorway stall count on this suite; it is finding a harder topology or completion task that exposes a real failure again. |
| QDR-315 | `tight_p1_cell_size_passability_probe` | QDR-314 showed the tight-p1 failure is not fixed by widening the planner clearance cone. Since tight gaps are only `0.34-0.50m`, is the `0.30m` exploration grid the wrong passability scale for the onboard planner? Compare nearby cell sizes on the same tight-p1 oracle stack before changing planner logic again. | Strong positive. GPU oracle sweep on tight-p1 seed `88`, QDR-311 flags, `2048` max steps: `cell=0.25` gave `73.33%` success / `26.67%` timeout; `0.30` gave `88.63%` / `11.37%`; `0.35` gave `96.63%` / `3.37%`; `0.40` regressed to `86.15%` / `13.85%`, all `0%` contacts. Held-out oracle `cell=0.35`: seed `90` `96.89%`, seed `91` `96.84%`, both `0%` contacts. Added `--ignore-checkpoint-env-config` so learned checkpoints can be evaluated with explicit env overrides; this is needed because checkpoints otherwise restore their saved `cell_size`. Existing QDR-312 u400 at `cell=0.35`, tight-p1 seeds `88/90/91`: `97.51/97.41/97.83%` success, `2.49/2.59/2.17%` timeout, `0%` contacts. No-render visual smoke: `32/32`, `0%` timeout/contact, `wall_stall_logs=88`. Broader QDR-312 u400 tight-family regression at `cell=0.35`: p0 `99.62/99.57/99.67%`, p2 `99.80/99.78/99.77%`, p3 `99.93/99.90/99.95%`, all `0%` contacts. QDR-312 eval/visual logs fetched to `logs/qdr315_remote/`; oracle sweep metrics are recorded here from GPU stdout. | Promote `cell_size=0.35` as the current tight-topology runtime/eval setting with QDR-312 u400. The missing piece was representation scale/passability, not a wider safety cone or more policy tuning: `0.30m` made tight gaps produce too much frontier/stale churn, `0.25m` was worse, and `0.40m` hid too much structure. The remaining p1 tail is about `2-3%` safe timeouts, so the next limit is late completion in the hardest tight pattern, not crashing. |
| QDR-314 | `tight_p1_oracle_timeout_diagnosis` | QDR-313 made `procedural_indoor_tight_p1` the next honest limit and showed even the oracle times out about `7-8%` with zero contacts. Why does the deployable onboard oracle fail late exploration completion in tight topology? Capture no-render visual/oracle stall traces, then change the planner only if the failure mode is specific. | Failed as a fix, useful as diagnosis. GPU no-render oracle visual, tight-p1 seed `88`, `32` episodes, `cell_size=0.30`, default planner cone, no visual restart: `90.62%` success / `9.38%` timeout / `0%` contacts, coverage `0.9121`, `wall_stall_logs=101`; timeout episodes were mid-coverage (`0.4821`, `0.4602`, `0.6260`). A restart-on-stall visual loop reached `100%` counted success but only by restarting early, so it is diagnostic, not a pass criterion. Added opt-in `--explore-onboard-clearance-cone-deg`; aligning planner cone to the safety cone at `60deg` regressed the same no-restart trace to `84.38%` success / `15.62%` timeout / `0%` contacts, coverage `0.9011`, `wall_stall_logs=106`. Logs are under `logs/qdr314_remote/`. | Do not promote planner/safety cone alignment. The target stream is not blind to walls (`angle_err=0`, rays/target cones report nearby geometry), and widening the planner cone makes the oracle more conservative without solving stale/late frontier completion. The next lever should be representation scale, target lifecycle, or a stronger topological completion objective. |
| QDR-313 | `harder_room_limit_probe` | QDR-312 showed the existing hard-p1 suite is saturated and mostly differentiates efficiency, not qualitatively new failures. What breaks first if we keep the same deployable actor observations/planner stack but enlarge/tighten the procedural layout? Compare QDR-300+QDR-311 runtime passage-quality against QDR-312 u400 before building another planner/training component. | Passed as a limit-finding diagnostic. GPU eval first tried `procedural_indoor_hard_p1`, room `8.0m`, max steps `4096`, goal fraction `0.95` then `0.98`, seeds `88/90/91`. That did not expose a meaningful limit: QDR-300+QDR-311 was `100/100/100%` success at both thresholds, zero contacts/timeouts; QDR-312 u400 was `99.93/100/100%` at `0.95` and `99.85/100/100%` at `0.98`, zero contacts with only a tiny seed-88 timeout tail. The useful change was adding `procedural_indoor_tight(_p0..p3)`: narrower gaps (`0.34-0.50m` instead of `0.46-0.64m`) plus extra clutter. CPU smoke proved tight-p1 is real pressure: oracle seed `88`, 30 episodes, `90.0%` success / `10.0%` timeout / `0%` contacts. GPU tight-p1 seeds `88/90/91`, room `6.0m`, max steps `2048`, goal fraction `0.95`: oracle `92.62/91.89/92.22%`, QDR-300+QDR-311 `93.84/93.42/94.89%`, QDR-312 u400 `94.27/94.61/94.19%`, all `0%` contacts. Logs fetched to `logs/qdr312_remote/qdr313_tight_p1_eval.log`. | Room scale alone is not enough; the old generator was topologically too easy. `procedural_indoor_tight_p1` is the next honest limit. The failure is safe timeout/late-frontier completion, not crashing. Because even the oracle is only about `92%`, the next build should improve the onboard planner/topological objective on tight-p1 before training more student checkpoints. |
| QDR-312 | `passage_quality_target_distill` | QDR-311 proved passage-quality centerline target selection fixes the remaining hard-family doorway stalls as a runtime planner wrapper. Can we train/distill that target distribution into the deployable target policy, starting from QDR-300, while preserving fixed-layout skill and zero-contact behavior? | Modest positive, with early-stop caveat. GPU run completed on `beeblebrox` with `scripts/quadrotor4_qdr312_passage_quality_distill.sh`. Remote build needed the script to set `CUDA_HOME=/usr/local/cuda`, venv `PATH`, and `CC=gcc CXX=g++` because the current repo no longer has `setup.py`, `python` was not on remote `PATH`, and `clang` was absent. The final checkpoint is safe but not the best: final in-memory eval was `99.51%` success / `0.49%` timeout / `0%` contacts, and 32-episode no-render visual had one timeout (`96.88%`, `wall_stall_logs=0`). Best candidate is early checkpoint `experiments/qdr312/checkpoints/qdr312_passage_quality_distill_s88_u000400.pt`. Fair hard-p1 drained eval vs QDR-300+QDR-311 runtime flags, seeds `88/90/91`, `1024` requested: baseline success `99.42/99.71/99.22%`, paths `149.445/147.239/149.940m`, new-cell `0.899/0.917/0.896`; QDR-312 u400 success `99.51/99.71/99.52%`, paths `148.552/145.010/148.999m`, new-cell `0.907/0.933/0.907`, all `0%` contacts. u400 no-render visual hard-p1 seed `88`, `32` episodes: `32/32`, `0%` contacts/timeouts, new-cell `0.9113`, but `wall_stall_logs=1`; QDR-300+QDR-311 visual remains cleaner (`32/32`, `wall_stall_logs=0`, new-cell `0.8569`). Expanded hard-family u400 regression on p0/p2/p3 seeds `88/90/91` stayed zero-contact and broadly matched baseline; tiny timeout tails exist in both policies under the larger drained sample. Checkpoints/logs fetched locally under `experiments/qdr312/` and `logs/qdr312_remote/`. | Distilling passage-quality targets is useful, but not a major new limit break. The gain is efficiency, not a dramatic success-rate jump: u400 is the strongest trained checkpoint, while QDR-300+QDR-311 remains the cleanest runtime visual reference. Do not promote the final u800 checkpoint; late student rollout (`student_prob` near `0.5`) reintroduces timeout drift. Next attempt should either early-stop/lower student rollout around u400 or, better, push both QDR-311 and QDR-312 u400 into a harder layout suite to expose the next real blocker. |
| QDR-311 | `onboard_passage_quality_centerline_bias` | QDR-310 showed binary passability is too crude. Can we preserve doorway graph connectivity while biasing frontier/viewpoint selection away from wall-edge cells and toward clearance-centered passage cells? Add opt-in passage-quality scoring, then gate on hard-p1 oracle before policy/GPU. | Promoted as the current hard-family runtime planner profile. Added default-off `--explore-onboard-passage-quality --explore-onboard-passage-weight <w>`. The implementation does not close cells; it adds a local clearance-center score to onboard frontier/viewpoint selection and triggers the existing local viewpoint selector when the emitted waypoint is a wall-edge cell. `py_compile` and CPU backend build passed. Oracle hard-p1 seed `88`, `256` episodes improved from the QDR-301/QDR-300 baseline `100%`, path `168.063m`, new-cell rate `0.788219`, min clearance `0.383617` to `100%`, path `150.994m`, new-cell rate `0.898366`, min clearance `0.387858`. Held-out oracle seeds also passed: seed `90` `100%`, path `143.825m`, new-cell rate `0.960436`; seed `91` `100%`, path `145.804m`, new-cell rate `0.935927`. QDR-300 policy hard-p1 eval with the same runtime flag passed seeds `88/90/91` at `100%`, `0%` contacts/timeouts, paths `148.253m` / `145.983m` / `147.259m`, new-cell rates `0.907516` / `0.926014` / `0.917715`. The 32-episode no-render visual loop improved to `32/32`, `0%` contacts/timeouts, new-cell rate `0.8545`, and `wall_stall_logs=0` versus prior `3`. Hard-family forced-pattern regression passed all `12/12` cases: p0/p1/p2/p3 across seeds `88/90/91`, all `100%`, all `0%` contacts/timeouts. Random `procedural_indoor_hard` mix also passed seeds `88/90/91`, all `100%`, all `0%` contacts/timeouts, coverage about `0.9536-0.9542`, paths `14.3m` / `19.25m` / `16.30m`. | This is the missing abstraction QDR-310 was reaching for: not binary passability, but passage quality. Keep gaps open, then prefer frontier/viewpoint cells nearer the passage centerline. The current best hard-family eval/visual profile is QDR-300 u100 + `--cell-size 0.30 --explore-onboard-passage-quality --explore-onboard-passage-weight 0.35`. Next step is to distill/train against this target distribution instead of relying on an eval-only planner wrapper. |
| QDR-310 | `onboard_clearance_passability_layer` | Do the remaining doorway stalls come from the onboard grid treating every visible frontier as traversable, even when the known blocked-cell geometry leaves less clearance than the drone/controller needs? Add an opt-in planner-only passability layer that inflates known blocked cells before onboard BFS/frontier selection, then compare hard-p1 oracle and policy visual/eval against QDR-300/QDR-301. | Failed the local oracle gate. Added default-off `--explore-onboard-clearance-passable --explore-onboard-passable-radius <m>` and checkpoint/config plumbing. `py_compile` and CPU backend build passed. With `--cell-size 0.30`, radius `0.30` was behavior-equivalent to the known QDR-301/QDR-300 oracle baseline (`100%`, coverage `0.950574`, path `168.063m`, min clearance `0.383617`) because it does not exceed one grid cell. Radius `0.31` or `0.35` was destructive: hard-p1 seed `88`, `256` episodes fell to `35.13%` success / `64.87%` timeout, coverage `0.6285`, new-cell rate `0.7014`, min clearance `0.4761`. Restricting the layer to known-cell BFS/path traversability only, leaving unknown frontier cells unfiltered, was still destructive at radius `0.31`: `30.47%` success / `69.53%` timeout, coverage `0.6138`, new-cell rate `0.3871`, min clearance `0.4082`. Combining a finer `0.20m` grid with radius `0.25` improved over the worst collapse but still failed (`57.81%` success / `42.19%` timeout at `128` episodes, coverage `0.9121`) and remained far below the `0.30m` baseline. No policy/GPU run was launched because the oracle ceiling regressed. | Do not promote simple binary passability inflation. On a `0.30m` grid it has no useful middle ground: below one cell it does nothing; above one cell it closes too much of the doorway graph. The user intuition is still useful, but the abstraction should be “passage quality/centerline” rather than “gap open/closed.” Next attempt should bias targets/waypoints toward doorway medial axes or clearance-weighted local passages while preserving passable gaps, not simply mark near-wall cells unreachable. |
| QDR-309 | `progress_aware_slide_projection` | Can we fix QDR-307's local slide over-projection without QDR-308's naive forward forcing? Add opt-in `--safety-slide-progress-aware` so slide interpolates back only toward the pre-slide command and only enough to preserve a fraction of pre-slide target progress when the target cone is passable. Gate on hard-p1 visual/eval before promotion. | Failed as a promotion, but useful as a diagnostic probe. Added opt-in `--safety-slide-progress-aware --safety-slide-progress-fraction <f> --safety-slide-progress-cone <m>`, default off. `py_compile` passed. The `f=0.60, cone=0.60` visual loop changed the two slide-erased stalls in the intended direction: progress ratios rose from `0.006/0.042` to `0.108/0.118`, with no contacts/timeouts, but 32-episode no-render visual still had `wall_stall_logs=3` and lower new-cell rate (`0.7565` vs QDR-307 baseline `0.7815`). Stronger `f=1.00` was destructive: visual fell to `93.75%` success with `6.25%` timeout and `6` stall logs. Oracle seed `88`, `256` episodes at `f=0.60` stayed effectively neutral (`100%`, `0%` contacts, path `168.006m`, min clearance `0.3820m` vs baseline `168.063m`/`0.3836m`). QDR-300 policy eval seed `88`, `512` requested episodes regressed slightly: `100%`, coverage `0.950560`, new-cell rate `0.758808`, path `171.731m`, min clearance `0.3721m` vs baseline `0.950551`, `0.761294`, `171.314m`, `0.3730m`. | Do not promote progress-aware slide in this form. Interpolating back toward the pre-slide command confirms the mechanism but does not improve policy efficiency; stronger restoration causes timeouts. The next attempt should not be another scalar restoration fraction. Use QDR-307 diagnostics to build a local controller that evaluates short closed-loop progress/clearance, or redesign slide to reason about corridor tangent and doorway passage geometry rather than preserving a fixed progress fraction. |
| QDR-308 | `post_slide_progress_floor_probe` | QDR-307 diagnostics show that slide can erase barrier-preserved target progress. Does the existing deployable post-safety progress floor (`--safety-progress-floor-scale 0.50 --safety-progress-floor-cone 0.60`) reduce stalls without contacts or efficiency loss? Test local visual, oracle, and policy eval before promoting. | Failed as a promotion. Oracle seed `88`, `256` episodes with QDR-301/QDR-300 stack stayed `100%` success / `0%` contacts and slightly shortened path (`167.363m` vs baseline `168.063m`) but lowered min clearance (`0.379m` vs `0.384m`). QDR-300 policy 32-episode no-render visual stayed safe but not better: `32/32`, `0%` contacts/timeouts, `wall_stall_logs=3`, new-cell rate `0.7706` vs baseline `0.7815`; one remaining stall had a genuinely blocked target cone (`target_cone_scale=0.096`) and the controller correctly moved away. Policy eval seed `88`, `512` requested episodes regressed slightly: baseline `100%`, coverage `0.950551`, new-cell rate `0.761294`, path `171.314m`, min clearance `0.3730m`; progress floor `100%`, coverage `0.950526`, new-cell rate `0.757832`, path `171.533m`, min clearance `0.3689m`. A temporary barrier-progress-floor prototype had no effect on the trace and was removed. | Do not promote simple post-slide progress forcing. It fixes the appearance of two slide-erased stall lines but does not improve policy efficiency and reduces clearance. The useful finding is diagnostic: slide can over-project, but naive forward forcing trades safety margin/efficiency and does not remove the stall count. Next local-control attempt should be a smarter slide/projection rule that preserves progress only when the forward cone has enough true clearance, or a short closed-loop local controller with an explicit clearance-progress tradeoff. |
| QDR-307 | `local_execution_stall_diagnosis` | Are the remaining QDR-300/QDR-306 near-wall stalls caused by topological target churn, raw targets pointing through unsafe doorway geometry, or the deployable local safety/controller layer slowing/rotating otherwise valid targets? Add targeted no-render visual diagnostics for raw desired action versus final action under the current barrier/slide/cone stack, then run the same 32-episode hard-p1 stall loop before changing controller behavior. | Passed as diagnosis. Added stall log fields for target cone scale, raw desired action, barrier-only action, slide-after-barrier action, final speed/progress ratios, and turn angle. Baseline QDR-300 policy hard-p1 seed `88`, 32-episode no-render visual stayed `32/32`, no contacts/timeouts, coverage `0.9505`, new-cell rate `0.7815`, `wall_stall_logs=3`. One stall was harmless-ish: target cone scale `0.952`, barrier progress ratio `0.866`, slide progress ratio `0.851`, final progress ratio `0.851`. Two stalls exposed the real local execution issue: target cone scale was still moderate (`0.698`/`0.761`), barrier preserved some progress (`0.181`/`0.197`) while turning `75deg`, but slide cut progress almost to zero (`0.006`/`0.042`) and turned the command `89deg`/`85deg`. Oracle ablation without slide remained `100%` safe but worsened path (`172.609m` vs slide baseline `168.063m`) and min clearance (`0.344m` vs `0.384m`), so removing slide is not the answer. | The remaining visible stalls are not primarily missing targets or topological region identity. They are local execution artifacts in tight geometry: barrier+slide is globally helpful, but slide can over-project a moderately valid target into near-zero-progress wall-parallel motion. Keep the diagnostics. Do not remove slide globally; improve its projection/progress tradeoff or test a better local controller. |
| QDR-306 | `passive_frontier_region_tracker` | Can we make QDR-305's persistent region idea safe by turning it into a passive diagnostic layer first? Add opt-in stable frontier-region tracking and HUD/log metrics without changing target selection or stale marking. Gate on behavior-equivalence against QDR-301/QDR-300 before using it for visual diagnosis. | Passed as instrumentation, not a planner promotion. Added `--explore-onboard-region-tracker`, native HUD fields, and eval/visual metrics (`region_tracker_regions`, `region_tracker_active_age`, `region_tracker_since_progress`, `region_tracker_switch_rate`, `region_tracker_stale_rate`). `py_compile` and CPU backend build passed. Behavior-equivalence gate on hard-p1 seed `88`, `256` episodes, QDR-301/QDR-300 stack: baseline and tracker both produced `100%` success, `0%` contacts/timeouts, coverage `0.950574`, path `168.063m`, min clearance `0.383617`, action delta `0.0640693`, new-cell rate `0.788219`. Tracker diagnostics on the same run: regions `15.4784`, active age `386.797`, since-progress `34.1852`, switch rate `0.0428793`, stale rate `0.0236302`. No-render visual with QDR-300 policy solved `32/32`, coverage `0.9505`, no contacts/timeouts, `wall_stall_logs=3`; tracker summary regions `14.6912`, active age `361.2634`, since-progress `33.8738`, switch rate `0.0473`, stale rate `0.0240`. | Keep the tracker passive by default. QDR-306 gives us a safe debug view for persistent region identity and switching, but it proves nothing about a better planner yet. The 32-episode stall-bearing sample still solved cleanly; the logged stalls often had valid/far onboard targets and low safety cone clearance, so the next diagnostic should separate topological region churn from local safety/controller slowdown before making region state active again. |
| QDR-305 | `persistent_frontier_region_manager` | Can a persistent onboard frontier-region manager improve over QDR-301/QDR-300 by giving frontier clusters stable identity, progress accounting, failed-attempt penalties, and commitment across reclustering? Build it opt-in, test oracle first on hard-p1 with QDR-300/QDR-301 settings, and only use GPU/policy if the oracle ceiling improves without contacts. | Failed hard locally, so GPU/policy runs were skipped. Added opt-in `--explore-onboard-persistent-regions`, C backend rebuild passed, and default-disabled baseline still works. Baseline hard-p1 seed `88`, `256` episodes, QDR-301/QDR-300 stack: `100%` success / `0%` contacts/timeouts, coverage `0.950574`, path `168.063m`. First active persistent manager collapsed to `59.93%` success / `40.07%` timeout, coverage `0.9196`, path `179.615m`, target age `15.35`. Stall-only overlay with active continuation was still bad: `62.16%` success / `37.84%` timeout, path `188.841m`. Removing active continuation worsened to `50.58%` success / `49.42%` timeout, path `216.688m`. Fixing the progress-clock bug exposed the real danger: stale-region churn collapsed to `13.67%` success / `86.33%` timeout and coverage `0.4797`. A no-flag 128-episode smoke after the changes stayed sane (`99.25%` success, `0%` contacts), so the failed path is isolated behind the flag. | Do not promote active persistent target selection. The direction is still conceptually right, but directly letting persistent region memory own target selection/stale marking is dangerous; it can suppress reachable frontiers and destroy coverage. The next attempt should first build a passive region tracker/debug probe that reports stable IDs, switches, progress, and stale decisions without changing targets. Only after the passive tracker predicts failures correctly should it influence scoring. |
| QDR-304 | `trajectory_valued_safety_controller_oracle` | Can we avoid repeating QDR-289/QDR-290/QDR-291 by testing a runtime deployable local trajectory controller directly in oracle eval, instead of generating hard-state labels or training a recovery sidecar? Add an opt-in action wrapper that scores short constant-velocity candidate headings against lidar clearance, rollout room, smoothness, and target progress. Gate it through hard-p1 oracle before any policy or GPU run. | Failed to clear the local oracle gate, so GPU/policy runs were skipped. Added opt-in `--safety-trajectory-actions`. Standalone trajectory controller on hard-p1 seed `88`, `256` episodes, QDR-301/QDR-300 stack: `100%` success / `0%` contacts/timeouts, but worse path `178.393m` vs baseline `168.063m`, lower new-cell rate `0.7711` vs `0.7882`, higher action delta `0.0900` vs `0.0641`. Gated standalone was still worse (`178.075m` path). Strict near-wall-only standalone was also worse (`1340.44` episode length, `178.174m` path). Stacking strict trajectory on top of barrier/slide was the only non-bad variant: seed `88` `100%`, path `167.801m`, new-cell rate `0.7891`; but held-out seed `90` regressed slightly versus baseline (`163.997m` path vs `163.744m`, `0.8064` vs `0.8076` new-cell rate). | Do not promote. This confirms the concern that we have been here before: short local heading/trajectory scoring over current rays mostly rephrases the existing barrier/slide controller. It is safe, but it does not robustly improve exploration efficiency. If local planning is revisited, it needs real region/topological progress value or learned/global memory, not another current-lidar constant-velocity candidate scorer. |
| QDR-303 | `frontier_region_approach_viewpoint` | Can the onboard planner improve beyond QDR-301 by proactively replacing shallow one-cell frontier targets with a clearance-friendly local approach viewpoint for the same frontier region? Add an opt-in planner flag, test oracle first on hard-p1 with QDR-300/QDR-301 settings, and only test the policy if the oracle improves without contacts. | Failed locally, so GPU/policy runs were skipped. Rebuilt the CPU backend and compared hard-p1 seed `88`, `256` episodes, `--cell-size 0.30`, QDR-301/QDR-300 oracle stack. Baseline: `100%` success, `0%` contacts/timeouts, coverage `0.950574`, episode length `1276.98`, path `168.063m`, min clearance `0.3836m`. `--explore-onboard-approach-viewpoints --explore-onboard-approach-max-depth 1`: `97.318%` success, `2.682%` timeout, `0%` contacts, coverage `0.947767`, episode length `1338.44`, path `189.375m`, min clearance `0.3975m`. Depth `2`: `98.099%` success, `1.901%` timeout, `0%` contacts, coverage `0.948679`, path `188.780m`, min clearance `0.4018m`. Existing late-stall viewpoint trigger at `90` ticks was neutral: `100%` success, `0%` contacts/timeouts, coverage `0.950568`, path `169.659m`. | Do not promote proactive local approach viewpoints. They buy a little clearance but turn an already-solving oracle into longer paths and timeouts. The shallow-target stall is not solved by replacing every near frontier with the existing local viewpoint selector. Keep the flag disabled by default; if revisited, the selector needs region-level commitment/trajectory value, not a blanket shallow-depth trigger. |
| QDR-302 | `cell030_rollout_matched_finetune` | Does QDR-301's `--cell-size 0.30` improvement survive rollout-matched training rather than only eval-time planner replacement? Start from `experiments/qdr300/checkpoints/qdr300_region_commit_barrier_ft_qdr285_s88_u000100.pt`, keep QDR-300 onboard target/region-commit/barrier/slide settings, train with `--cell-size 0.30`, checkpoint early, then compare hard-p1 seeds `88/90/91` and retention. | Failed as a promotion. Patched target-distill checkpoint config to save/load `cell_size`, then trained `experiments/qdr302/qdr302_cell030_ft_qdr300_s88.pt` for `150` updates with checkpoints every `25`, anchored to QDR-300 on easy/fixed modes. Best seed-88 saved checkpoint was u100: `99.1713%` success / `0.8287%` timeout / `0%` contacts. Other seed-88 checkpoints were safe but no better: u25 `99.0724%`, u50 `99.0792%`, u75 `98.8930%`, u125 `98.7793%`, u150/final `98.9720%`. Held-out u100: seed `90` `98.8062%` / `1.1938%` timeout / `0%` contacts, seed `91` `98.6188%` / `1.3812%` timeout / `0%` contacts. Strongest failed checkpoint fetched locally to `experiments/qdr302/checkpoints/qdr302_cell030_ft_qdr300_s88_u000100.pt`. | Do not promote rollout-matched QDR-302. The fine-tune is safe, but it does not beat QDR-301's simpler QDR-300 u100 + `--cell-size 0.30` eval profile (`99.2572%`/`98.6098%`/`99.0619%` on seeds `88/90/91`). Retention was not run because the hard-p1 promotion gate failed. The current best remains QDR-300 u100 with explicit `--cell-size 0.30`. The next improvement should change planner semantics or train objective, not lightly distill the already-good policy against the coarser grid. |
| QDR-301 | `near_stall_grid_size_diagnosis` | Do the remaining QDR-300 doorway `near_wall_stall` events come from the exploration grid being too coarse/fine, or from planner/controller semantics around tiny local targets? Compare onboard oracle/planner traces on hard-p1 with the QDR-300 full-stack settings while changing only `--cell-size`, then test QDR-300 u100 under the same settings. | Strong positive for coarser cells. Local one-episode planner traces: `0.20m` timed out at `81.7%` coverage, while `0.25/0.30/0.35m` solved the same seed. GPU oracle seed `88`, 1024-ish episodes: `0.20m` `52.83%` success / `47.17%` timeout; `0.25m` `96.18%` / `3.82%`; `0.30m` `100%` / `0%`; `0.35m` `100%` / `0%`, all `0%` contacts. QDR-300 u100 policy eval: seed `88` at `0.25m` `95.15%` / `4.85%`, `0.30m` `99.26%` / `0.74%`, `0.35m` `98.68%` / `1.32%`, all `0%` contacts. Held-out `0.30m`: seed `90` `98.61%` / `1.39%`, seed `91` `99.06%` / `0.94%`, all `0%` contacts. Visual no-render smoke over 3 episodes: `0.25m` logged `2` near-wall stalls with onboard distance exactly one cell; `0.30m` logged `0`. Added visual diagnostics for `target_dist_m`, `onboard_dist_m`, target distance in cell units, and total `wall_stall_logs`. | The hard-p1 grid was too fine, not too coarse. `0.20m` creates more cells/frontiers/stale marks and makes tiny local target churn worse. `0.30m` is the best current hard-p1 runtime setting: it reduces one-cell doorway objectives enough to remove most near-wall stalls without hiding the task behind contacts or excessive caution. Do not test finer grids again unless the planner semantics change. Next step should fine-tune/train with `--cell-size 0.30` in the rollout loop and then verify broader procedural/fixed-layout retention. |
| QDR-300 | `region_commit_barrier_rollout_finetune` | Does QDR-299 need policy distribution matching rather than eval-time wrapping? Hypothesis: QDR-299 raises the oracle ceiling slightly, but QDR-285 policy drops when the native region-commit planner and barrier controller are applied only at eval. Fine-tune QDR-285 with `--explore-onboard-region-commit --explore-onboard-region-commit-stall-ticks 90 --safety-barrier-actions --safety-slide` in the rollout loop, then evaluate hard-p1. Command trained from `experiments/qdr285/qdr285_fast_safe_rollout_qdr282_s88.pt` for `300` updates with hard-p1/hard-p3-heavy modes plus anchors; checkpoints under `experiments/qdr300/checkpoints/`. | Small promotion, not solved. Hard-p1 seed `88`, full 1024 eval with full stack (`--explore-onboard-region-commit --explore-onboard-region-commit-stall-ticks 90 --safety-barrier-actions --safety-slide --safety-stateful-recovery --recovery-stall-steps 96 --recovery-duration-steps 64`) reached `96.9844%` success / `3.0156%` timeout / `0%` contacts. Held-out seeds: seed `90` `96.1427%` / `3.8573%` timeout / `0%` contacts; seed `91` `95.6268%` / `4.3732%` timeout / `0%` contacts. Local no-render visual smoke passed `3/3`, `0%` contacts/timeouts, `95.03%` coverage, but still logged recoverable near-wall stalls. | Promote QDR-300 u100 as the current hard-p1 candidate because it improves held-out seeds over QDR-285 while preserving zero contacts. Do not continue longer fine-tuning blindly; u200 and u300 regressed (`93.87%` and `95.61%` on 512-ish eval). The remaining limit is still the doorway/near-wall stall pattern, so the next build should add deeper topological progress/state rather than another long fine-tune. |
| QDR-299 | `onboard_region_goal_commit` | Can we fix the remaining doorway target flipping at the native planner level instead of wrapping actions? Hypothesis: QDR-296/QDR-298 traces show flat coverage with tiny adjacent targets that flip inside the same doorway frontier component. Add opt-in `--explore-onboard-region-commit` so the onboard planner keeps the chosen frontier goal within a connected region until that goal becomes invalid/stale, rather than reselecting the best adjacent frontier every frame. Compare onboard oracle hard-p1 against QDR-295/QDR-297. | Small positive only when gated to stalls and stacked with the controller. Hard-locking the first region goal was rejected immediately: local CPU smoke fell to `67.69%` success / `32.31%` timeout. Score-gap hysteresis without a stall gate was still worse than baseline (`93.75%` vs `96.92%` local 64-episode A/B). Adding `--explore-onboard-region-commit-stall-ticks 90` fixed the local regression (`98.44%` local 64 episodes vs `96.92%` baseline), but the GPU medium oracle was only a small gain (`95.2381%` vs matching baseline `94.8473%`, zero contacts). The best stack, region commit + QDR-297 barrier/slide/stateful recovery, reached `97.6923%` on a 512-ish eval and `96.9142%` on the full 1024 eval, zero contacts. This is the best oracle ceiling so far, but applying the same stack to QDR-285 policy gave only `95.5939%` on a 512-ish eval. | Native region-goal hysteresis is useful only as a late-stall stabilizer, not a hard planner lock. It raises the oracle ceiling slightly and stacks with barrier/recovery, but it is not a drop-in policy wrapper. Next step is train/fine-tune with this planner/controller in the rollout loop; if that fails, the remaining limit is deeper topological region search, not more hysteresis thresholds. |
| QDR-298 | `close_waypoint_objective_drive_gate` | QDR-296/QDR-297 traces show the remaining doorway stalls often have a valid onboard target with tiny distance (`~0.02`) that flips around a doorway while coverage is flat. Add an opt-in deployable gate that, only when the local target is very close, drives the action toward the deeper onboard objective exposed by `--explore-onboard-objective-obs`. This differs from QDR-293 because it does not replace the main target globally. | Failed as a promotion. Local smoke was safe but unchanged. GPU hard-p1 seed `88`, `512`-episode checks: objective gate + barrier + slide reached `96.5583%` success / `3.4417%` timeout / `0%` contacts, only a tiny improvement over QDR-296-style barrier and not enough to justify the extra semantic branch. Objective gate + slide without barrier was bad: `93.9163%` success / `6.0837%` timeout / `0%` contacts and much older target age. | The close-waypoint/deeper-objective theory is plausible, but this simple action-side gate is not the missing piece. Do not use objective-drive without the barrier. If revisited, it needs native planner state/region identity, not a Python action wrapper that swaps direction based only on target distance. |
| QDR-297 | `local_barrier_steering_threshold_ablation` | The first barrier controller improved QDR-295 but may be too conservative about when to prioritize wall escape. Hypothesis: doorway stalls are triggered with side clearance around `0.30-0.36m`, but acting only below `0.36m` may be late; sweep the deployable near-wall gate and slide/no-slide composition before deciding whether this controller family is worth training into the actor. | Mixed; useful but not a policy promotion. Barrier + slide + existing stateful recovery (`--recovery-stall-steps 96 --recovery-duration-steps 64`) was best on a 512-ish eval (`97.1209%`, `0%` contacts), but the full 1024 eval settled at only `96.8023%` / `3.1977%` timeout / `0%` contacts. Applying the same wrapper to QDR-285 policy did not transfer: 1024 eval dropped to `95.3488%`; a 512 barrier-only policy check dropped to `94.4338%` versus matching baseline `96.3947%`. | Barrier steering plus stateful recovery can raise the onboard oracle ceiling slightly and safely, but the learned policy is not distribution-matched to that wrapper. Do not promote it as an eval-time policy wrapper. If used, train with the controller in the rollout loop, but the small oracle gain suggests the larger next step should be native planner/topological progress accounting rather than deeper tuning of this Python steering layer. |
| QDR-296 | `local_barrier_steering_oracle_probe` | Can we raise the deployable onboard planner/controller ceiling without another policy fine-tune? | QDR-292 | `late_stall_adjacent_viewpoint_trigger` | Can we reduce QDR-285/QDR-291 doorway stalls by fixing the emitted local objective, not by learning another recovery action? Hypothesis: visual traces show no-progress states with tiny adjacent frontier targets where cone clearance is not low enough to trigger the existing local-viewpoint selector. Added opt-in `--explore-onboard-stall-viewpoint-ticks` / `--explore-onboard-stall-viewpoint-max-depth` so shallow no-progress targets can force the existing deployable local-viewpoint selector. Main comparison: QDR-285 hard-p1 seed `88`, speed `0.50`, path lookahead `2`, slide safety, default vs `--explore-onboard-stall-viewpoint-ticks 120 --explore-onboard-stall-viewpoint-max-depth 1`. | Failed cleanly. Local structural smoke showed the new code path compiles and can affect pure planner-oracle stats, but it does not affect this pretrained policy rollout. | Do not continue the stall-timer trigger as a main path.
