| QDR-291 | `doorway_recovery_resettable_replay` | Can we turn the remaining doorway near-wall stall into a fast resettable benchmark before training another sidecar? Hypothesis: if QDR-288/QDR-290 failed because one-step or sparse BC labels miss closed-loop recovery, then replaying identical captured recovery-trigger states should separate base, hand-coded stateful recovery, learned sidecars, and future RL recovery policies by coverage gain, contacts, and timeout/open rate. Actor inputs remain deployable; simulator snapshots are used only for diagnostics/training resets. Main commands used `explore-v0-recovery-replay` on `experiments/qdr285/qdr285_fast_safe_rollout_qdr282_s88.pt` with hard-p1, speed `0.50`, path lookahead `2`, slide safety, and `128` captured states. Also added a base-action BC warm start before resettable policy-gradient recovery (`--bc-warmup-target base`) so the PG sidecar starts as a near-no-op instead of random actions. | Useful negative result. The replay harness works and is fast on GPU, but it does not support the current action-sidecar direction. Early-trigger states (`--recovery-stall-steps 96`) were not hard enough: base and stateful were tied over `128` restored states (`mean_gain=0.220587` vs `0.220744`, no contacts). Late-stall states (`--recovery-stall-steps 240`) were sharper: base beat stateful on replay coverage gain (`0.106726` vs `0.088603`, no contacts). Exact same-setting full eval on seed `88`: base `95.0476%` success / `4.952%` timeout / `0%` contacts; stateful `94.4656%` / `5.534%` / `0%`; base-warmed PG sidecar `94.9020%` / `5.098%` / `0%`. The un-warmed random PG sidecar was catastrophic (`17.95%` success), and the base-warmed PG sidecar still lost the replay metric (`mean_gain=0.047990`). | Do not keep optimizing the QDR-288/QDR-291 recovery-action sidecar as the main lever. Exact resettable replay shows the base controller already does as well or better than stateful recovery from these captured states, and simple PG mostly learns to interfere. The next useful build should either filter for truly low-gain/base-failing restored states before training, or move back up a level to local target/viewpoint/planner quality. The current blocker is not "choose a slightly different velocity during recovery"; it is that the emitted frontier/viewpoint objective still creates slow doorway progress. |
| QDR-290 | `doorway_recovery_trajectory_labels` | Does resettable hard-state recovery need trajectory-level labels rather than one-step MPC labels, and can the labeler be made fast enough? Extended `explore-v0-recovery-policy-train` with `--label-source trajectory`. Initial Python receding search was too slow, so the search was vectorized through the native C `StaticVec`: restore one hard state into `--search-agents` env slots, roll candidate actions together with `cpu_vec_step`, and score the short trajectories as a batch. Actor inputs remain deployable; privilege is used only to generate labels from restored simulator states. Main medium command: `PYTHON=.venv/bin/python scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-recovery-policy-train experiments/qdr285/qdr285_fast_safe_rollout_qdr282_s88.pt --output experiments/qdr290/qdr290_vectorized_trajectory_recovery_medium.pt --label-source trajectory --collect-steps 6000 --max-samples 64 --epochs 40 --batch-size 32 --recovery-hidden-size 256 --learning-rate 0.0007 --weight-decay 0.0 --print-interval 10 --eval-after --episodes 512 --total-agents 512 --num-buffers 1 --num-threads 8 --max-eval-steps 50000 --mpc-rollout-steps 4 --mpc-speed-fractions 1.0,0.65,0.35 --trajectory-steps 8 --trajectory-stop-gain 0.006 --trajectory-min-best-gain 0.001 --trajectory-min-clearance 0.22 --search-agents 96`. | Engineering pass, policy fail. Throughput improved substantially: a GPU-box smoke collected `8` accepted trajectory labels in `18s`, and the medium run collected `64` labels by `collect_step=1664` in a few minutes (`1290` filtered). The trained sidecar is not promotable. Before constraining learned recovery to XY only, eval was `87.60%` success with `3.44%` crashes. After the XY-only sidecar fix, crashes disappeared but performance remained bad: `n=524`, `87.60%` success, `12.40%` timeout, `0%` contacts, coverage `92.90%`. | Moving candidate rollout into the native vector env fixed the throughput bottleneck enough for diagnostics, but trajectory imitation is still the wrong objective or label distribution. Do not promote this checkpoint. Keep the vectorized search machinery, keep the XY-only recovery-sidecar safety fix, and move to a true resettable hard-state RL/replay benchmark or stronger trajectory optimizer; short sparse trajectory labels from doorway states are too biased and timeout-prone. |
| QDR-289 | `doorway_recovery_search_labels` | Does the learned recovery sidecar need stronger labels rather than more capacity? Added a `--label-source search` path to `explore-v0-recovery-policy-train` that captures QDR-285 doorway stall states, restores them into a one-agent env, and labels the sidecar with short-horizon local search actions scored by coverage gain, best coverage, clearance, and crash avoidance. This uses simulator/planner privilege for labels only; actor inputs remain deployable. Main probe: `PYTHON=../../.venv/bin/python scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-recovery-policy-train experiments/qdr285/qdr285_fast_safe_rollout_qdr282_s88.pt --output experiments/qdr289/qdr289_search_recovery_sidecar_probe.pt --cpu --label-source search --collect-steps 8000 --max-samples 256 --epochs 40 --batch-size 128 --recovery-hidden-size 256 --learning-rate 0.0007 --weight-decay 0.0 --print-interval 10 --eval-after --episodes 256 --total-agents 128 --num-buffers 1 --num-threads 8 --max-eval-steps 30000 --mpc-rollout-steps 16 --mpc-speed-fractions 1.0,0.65,0.35`. | Not promotable. The unfiltered 256-sample search sidecar looked promising on the small eval (`n=259`, `97.30%` success, `2.70%` timeout, `0%` contacts), but the same checkpoint dropped on the matching 512-episode drained eval (`n=529`, `95.84%` success, `3.97%` timeout, `0.19%` ground crash). No-render visual remained safe in 8 episodes but still logged doorway stalls. A stricter filter requiring short-horizon coverage gain and clearance was stopped because it accepted only `2/93` triggered states by step `768`; the label source itself rarely proved immediate coverage gain. | The right direction is stronger supervision, but naive first-action constant-control search is the wrong label abstraction. Do not repeat this exact MPC-label sidecar or loosen it blindly. The next build should optimize full recovery trajectories from captured hard states, or train/evaluate a local recovery policy directly in a resettable hard-state replay task, because useful doorway recovery may need several setup actions before coverage changes. |
| QDR-288 | `learned_doorway_recovery_sidecar` | Can we replace the hand-coded stateful recovery action with a small deployable learned local-action sidecar while keeping the base QDR-285 target policy fixed? Implemented `explore-v0-recovery-policy-train`, `--recovery-policy` eval/visual support, and a small MLP over deployable actor input, actor target, base action, and recovery internal state. Main command: `PYTHON=../../.venv/bin/python scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-recovery-policy-train experiments/qdr285/qdr285_fast_safe_rollout_qdr282_s88.pt --output experiments/qdr288/qdr288_recovery_sidecar_stateful_side.pt --cpu --collect-steps 12000 --max-samples 2048 --epochs 60 --batch-size 256 --recovery-hidden-size 256 --learning-rate 0.0007 --weight-decay 0.0 --print-interval 15 --eval-after --episodes 512 --total-agents 512 --num-buffers 1 --num-threads 8 --max-eval-steps 50000`. | Safe but not promotable. Raw learned recovery actions caused contacts until wrapped with the deployable slide/cone safety layer. Safe clones were contact-free but did not beat the doorway plateau. Best side-aware sidecar: drained hard-p1 seed `88`, `n=526`, `96.58%` success, `3.42%` timeout, `0%` contacts, coverage `94.76%`. No-render visual: `8/8` success, `0%` contacts/timeouts, coverage `95.03%`, but still logged doorway stalls. | The recovery interface is learnable and checkpoint-compatible, but cloning the current stateful heuristic only inherits its ceiling. Do not spend GPU on more MLP capacity or longer teacher imitation for this label source. Next serious rung needs better labels/objective: short-horizon hard-state search, privileged local recovery labels, or a module that directly optimizes leaving doorway stall states while preserving slide safety. |
| QDR-287 | `doorway_pathlookahead_and_recovery_probe` | Can deployable local-control/planner scaffolds reduce QDR-285's doorway near-wall stalls without changing the actor observation or checkpoint? Main repro: `explore-v0-target-visual experiments/qdr285/qdr285_fast_safe_rollout_qdr282_s88.pt --no-render --episodes 8 --mode procedural_indoor_hard_p1 --seed 88 --max-steps 2048 --room-size 6.0 --explore-goal-fraction 0.95 --spawn-radius-min 0.0 --speed-fraction 0.50 --explore-onboard-path-lookahead 2 --safety-slide --safety-cone-ray-stop 0.30 --safety-cone-deg 60 --safety-slow-ray 0.80 --restart-on-stall-steps 3000 --wall-stall-log-interval 1`. Probes included path lookahead `2/3`, stateful recovery, sampled safety, current-cell recentering, shallow-depth stall retargeting, clearance-centering, target-distance speed scaling, and action hysteresis. | Partial pass, not solved. Path lookahead `2` improves hard-p1 deterministic seed `88` from about `91.70%` to `94-96%` depending on eval drain/count, with zero contacts, and passes fixed smoke checks; lookahead `3` is not better. No-render visual with lookahead `2` still logs doorway stalls. Adding `--safety-stateful-recovery --recovery-stall-steps 96 --recovery-duration-steps 64` reduces the 8-episode visual stall trace substantially while preserving `8/8` success and zero contacts. Deterministic hard-p1 seed `88` after wiring recovery into eval: no recovery `n=1056`, `96.31%` success, `3.69%` timeout, `0%` contacts; recovery `n=1055`, `96.40%` success, `3.60%` timeout, `0%` contacts. Sampled safety produced many more stall logs. Shallow-depth stall retargeting made visual success collapse to `4/8` by skipping necessary local doorway work, so it was removed. | The immediate stalls are not a wall-vision failure and not fixed by skipping shallow frontiers. The best current deployable eval/visual profile is QDR-285 + path lookahead `2` + slide safety; stateful recovery is useful for visual/local recovery but only a small aggregate eval gain. Do not repeat clearance centering, target-distance speed scaling, action hysteresis, sampled safety, current-cell recentering, longer commitment, or shallow-depth retargeting without new evidence. Next serious build should teach or replace the recovery behavior with a learned/deployable local planner/action interface, not add another hand-tuned doorway heuristic. |
| QDR-286 | `doorway_target_churn_probe` | Can diagnostics explain QDR-285's visual doorway near-wall stalls? Repro traces on hard-p1 seed `88` show the policy target and onboard planner target agree closely, safety often allows full speed, target/onboard distance is frequently tiny, and coverage stays flat near a doorway frame. Tested whether existing onboard viewpoint commitment removes the issue. | Diagnostic pass, local commitment fail. `--explore-onboard-commit-steps 24` and `48` worsened the no-render visual smoke into timeouts (`75%` and `50%` success in 8-episode checks). Velocity-controller `Kp` increases did not solve the symptom; aggressive values were destructive. The trace falsifies the simple "policy cannot see the wall" theory because actor/onboard target agreement is high and rays report the doorway. | The failure is local frontier/doorway efficiency: the planner/controller can get pinned while consuming tiny current/adjacent frontier targets around a bottleneck. Commitment length and controller gain are not the right levers. Keep the rich `near_wall_stall` logging, and judge future fixes by visual stall traces plus drained hard-p1 eval, not just final success. |
| QDR-285 | `fast_safe_rollout_training` | Can training QDR-282 with the QDR-284 fast-safe controller profile in the rollout loop reduce the remaining hard-p1 `2048`-step timeout tail, rather than only fixing eval-time control? GPU command trained from `experiments/qdr282/qdr282_onboard_target_obs_qdr251_s88.pt` with old-mode anchors, hard-p1/hard-p3-heavy modes, `--learning-rate 0.00005`, `--seq-updates 800`, `--teacher-rollout-updates 80`, `--max-student-rollout-prob 0.5`, and fast-safe rollout profile `--speed-fraction 0.50 --safety-slide --safety-cone-ray-stop 0.30 --safety-cone-deg 60 --safety-slow-ray 0.80`. Output: `experiments/qdr285/qdr285_fast_safe_rollout_qdr282_s88.pt`. Visual command: `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-target-visual experiments/qdr285/qdr285_fast_safe_rollout_qdr282_s88.pt --mode procedural_indoor_hard_p1 --seed 88 --max-steps 2048 --room-size 6.0 --explore-goal-fraction 0.95 --spawn-radius-min 0.0 --speed-fraction 0.50 --safety-slide --safety-cone-ray-stop 0.30 --safety-cone-deg 60 --safety-slow-ray 0.80 --restart-on-stall-steps 3000`. | Meaningful progress, with a caveat. Local smoke passed, GPU train completed on `beeblebrox`, checkpoint fetched locally. Final in-memory eval during training was pessimistic (`75.83%` success / `24.17%` timeout) because it used active rollout/eval state; saved deterministic drained eval is the fair result. Checkpoint sweep hard-p1 seed `88`: u200 `93.73%`, u400 `93.96%`, u600 `94.56%`, u800/final `94.79%`, all `0%` contacts. Held-out hard-p1 final seeds `90/91`: `92.70%` / `92.12%`, all `0%` contacts. Average hard-p1 moves from QDR-284's `90.86%` to QDR-285's `93.20%`. Regression smoke seed `88`: procedural aggregate `99.14%`, layout_mixed `98.94%`, maze `97.90%`, corridor `99.24%`, all zero contacts. No-render hard-p1 visual smoke passed `3/3` with `95.02%` coverage, zero contacts/timeouts, but still logged recoverable near-wall stalls. | Promote QDR-285 as the new hard-p1 fast-exploration checkpoint, but keep the caveat: it improves the strict hard-p1 timeout tail without contacts, while slightly reducing easy/fixed retention, especially maze timeout rate. The missing piece is still local/frontier efficiency near walls, not basic flight or wall perception. Next step should target the remaining slow/stall tail with trajectory-level local motion or high-level frontier/path efficiency, not more raw speed. |
| QDR-284 | `qdr282_fast_exploration_rate_probe` | Can we improve QDR-282 hard-p1 `2048`-step success by safely increasing exploration rate, or is the limit planner/path efficiency rather than speed cap? Hypothesis: if the learned/onboard target stack is now safe enough, modest speed increases plus deployable slide safety should reduce timeouts without creating contacts; if contacts rise or success stays flat, the next build needs better frontier/path ordering rather than higher speed. Main commands were QDR-282 hard-p1 evals with `--drain-initial-episodes`, `--max-steps 2048`, `--room-size 6.0`, `--explore-goal-fraction 0.95`, and the speed/safety variants below. Promoted eval profile: `--speed-fraction 0.50 --safety-slide --safety-cone-ray-stop 0.30 --safety-cone-deg 60 --safety-slow-ray 0.80`. Visual smoke command: `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-target-visual experiments/qdr282/qdr282_onboard_target_obs_qdr251_s88.pt --mode procedural_indoor_hard_p1 --seed 88 --max-steps 2048 --room-size 6.0 --explore-goal-fraction 0.95 --spawn-radius-min 0.0 --speed-fraction 0.50 --safety-slide --safety-cone-ray-stop 0.30 --safety-cone-deg 60 --safety-slow-ray 0.80 --restart-on-stall-steps 3000`. | Passed as a controller-profile improvement, not a new checkpoint. Baseline QDR-282 seed `88` at `2048` was about `67.6%` success / `32.4%` timeout / `0%` obstacle in the local drained eval. Speed `0.45` without slide gave `73.05%` / `26.95%` / `0%`; speed `0.55` without slide kept success flat at `73.08%` but introduced `5.0%` obstacle contacts. Speed `0.45` plus slide gave `87.21%`, `89.23%`, `85.71%` on hard-p1 seeds `88/90/91`, all `0%` obstacle. The best conservative setting was speed `0.50` plus slide with earlier slowdown (`0.30/0.80`): hard-p1 seeds `88/90/91` were `88.76%`, `93.54%`, `90.27%`, all `0%` obstacle; new-cell rate improved to about `0.92-0.98` and min clearance stayed about `0.31m`. Regression smoke with that profile: procedural aggregate seed `88` `99.84%`, layout_mixed `99.12%`, maze `100%`, corridor `99.02%`, all zero contacts. No-render hard-p1 visual smoke passed `3/3`, zero contacts/timeouts. | The speed cap was a real part of the `2048`-step tail, but raw speed alone is unsafe or ineffective. The deployable slide safety layer is the missing control scaffold for faster exploration: it lets us raise speed while preserving clearance. Promote QDR-282 plus the QDR-284 fast-safe controller profile as the current hard-p1 eval/visual default. The next limit is the remaining `~6-11%` hard-p1 timeout tail at `2048`; do not keep pushing raw speed. Next build should optimize frontier/path efficiency or train with the fast-safe controller in the loop. |
| QDR-283 | `hybrid_sota_onboard_stall_path` | Can the QDR-282 timeout tail be attacked in the same architectural direction as FUEL/TARE/Active-Neural-SLAM style systems: explicit deployable onboard planner state plus learned control, rather than more PPO tuning? Hypothesis: the remaining hard-p1 failures should classify into planner-target failures, local wall-stall control failures, or policy-target usage failures. Commands: `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-onboard-hardstate-probe --cpu --no-run-mpc --hard-states 64 --collect-steps 60000 --replay-horizon 512 --mode procedural_indoor_hard_p1 --seed 88 --total-agents 64 --num-buffers 1 --num-threads 8 --max-steps 4096 --max-eval-steps 60000 --room-size 6.0 --explore-goal-fraction 0.98 --spawn-radius-min 0.0 --speed-fraction 0.35 --safety-cone-ray-stop 0.25 --safety-cone-deg 60 --safety-slow-ray 0.70`; `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-target-hardstate-probe experiments/qdr282/qdr282_onboard_target_obs_qdr251_s88.pt --cpu --hard-states 64 --rewind 128 --replay-horizon 512 --max-collect-steps 60000 --mode procedural_indoor_hard_p1 --seed 88 --total-agents 64 --num-buffers 1 --num-threads 8 --max-steps 2048 --max-eval-steps 60000 --room-size 6.0 --explore-goal-fraction 0.95 --spawn-radius-min 0.0 --speed-fraction 0.35 --safety-cone-ray-stop 0.25 --safety-cone-deg 60 --safety-slow-ray 0.70 --use-pred-vector-fallback --pred-vector-fallback-primary-threshold 0.6 --pred-vector-fallback-min-coverage 0.25 --pred-vector-fallback-min-dist 0.02`; horizon command added: `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-target-horizon-probe experiments/qdr282/qdr282_onboard_target_obs_qdr251_s88.pt --horizons 2048,4096 --seeds 88,90,91 --episodes 256`. | Progress, and it changes the interpretation. Onboard-oracle stall replay collected `64` near-wall/no-progress states in `2554` steps: mean coverage `0.571`, min ray `0.337m`, target distance `0.449`, action speed `0.179`. Baseline onboard-target actions from those states solved only `2/64` within `512` replay steps, but gained `0.0618` mean coverage, reached `0.0924` mean best gain, and had `0` crashes. QDR-282 timeout hard-state probe collected only `19` timeout states over `60000` steps; at rewind `128`, student and oracle targets were close (`7.47deg` mean angle, both target-exists `1.0`, sector top-match `0.947`). Student replay timed out `19/19`; oracle replay rescued `9/19`, still with `0` crashes. Horizon eval is the main finding: QDR-282 hard-p1 seed `88` at `2048` steps was `67.58%` success / `32.42%` timeout / `0%` obstacle over `256` requested drained eval, while `4096` steps was `98.08%` success / `1.92%` timeout / `0%` obstacle. Held-out `4096` checks: seed `90` `97.19%`, seed `91` `98.80%`, zero contacts. Added `episode_length`/`path_length` printing and `explore-v0-target-horizon-probe`; smoke probe reproduced `68.75%` at `2048` vs `98.04%` at `4096`, zero contacts. | The current QDR-282/QDR-283 system is not mostly blind or unsafe; it is slow and locally inefficient under a short horizon. Treat `2048`-step success as a fast-exploration metric, not the only exploration validity gate. Next work should optimize time-to-coverage/coverage-AUC/new-cell rate while preserving zero contacts, using SOTA-like frontier/path efficiency ideas. Do not keep adding target labels to fix a perception failure that the hard-state data does not support. |
| QDR-282 | `deployable_onboard_target_obs_scaffold` | Can we move from pure recurrent target prediction to a research-aligned planner-plus-learned-controller scaffold by exposing only deployable onboard planner targets to the actor? Hypothesis: QDR-281 failed because privileged region labels do not give the actor enough deployable spatial state. A target computed by the onboard frontier planner is deployable and may let the network focus on following/smoothing planner guidance instead of learning the whole frontier graph from raw history. GPU command: `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-target-distill --output experiments/qdr282/qdr282_onboard_target_obs_qdr251_s88.pt --init-checkpoint experiments/qdr251/qdr251_qdr224_spawn_p1p3_anchor_s88.pt --anchor-checkpoint experiments/qdr251/qdr251_qdr224_spawn_p1p3_anchor_s88.pt --anchor-modes procedural_indoor_p0 procedural_indoor_p2 maze center_block doorway corridor random_blocks layout_mixed --anchor-loss-weight 0.5 --deploy-patch-obs --deploy-onboard-target-obs --explore-reachable-coverage --explore-frontier-target-mode onboard --predict-frontier-sectors --predict-frontier-vector --frontier-sector-loss-weight 2.0 --frontier-vector-loss-weight 1.0 --frontier-vector-dist-loss-weight 2.0 --unclamped-frontier-vector-dist-loss --use-pred-vector-fallback --pred-vector-fallback-primary-threshold 0.6 --pred-vector-fallback-min-coverage 0.25 --pred-vector-fallback-min-dist 0.02 --train-modes procedural_indoor_hard_p1 procedural_indoor_hard_p1 procedural_indoor_hard_p1 procedural_indoor_hard_p1 procedural_indoor_hard_p3 procedural_indoor_hard_p3 procedural_indoor_p1 procedural_indoor_p3 procedural_indoor maze center_block doorway corridor random_blocks --mode procedural_indoor_hard_p1 --seed 88 --seq-updates 800 --teacher-rollout-updates 80 --max-student-rollout-prob 0.5 --learning-rate 0.0001 --total-agents 1024 --num-buffers 2 --num-threads 16 --max-steps 2048 --speed-fraction 0.35 --spawn-radius-min 0.0 --explore-goal-fraction 0.95 --safety-cone-ray-stop 0.25 --safety-cone-deg 60 --safety-slow-ray 0.70 --checkpoint-interval 200 --checkpoint-dir experiments/qdr282/checkpoints --print-interval 100 --eval-after --episodes 4096`. | Strongest current hard-p1 candidate, not solved. Local smoke and GPU train completed; checkpoint fetched locally to `experiments/qdr282/qdr282_onboard_target_obs_qdr251_s88.pt`. Initial high-parallel eval falsely looked like `100%` because eval stopped after enough fast successes and censored slow timeouts. Added `--drain-initial-episodes` to force vector eval to run at least one full episode horizon before stopping. Corrected drained hard-p1 seed `88`, `512` requested episodes: QDR-251 baseline `28.16%` success / `71.84%` timeout / `0%` obstacle; QDR-282 `75.51%` / `24.49%` timeout / `0%` obstacle. Held-out drained hard-p1: seed `90` `76.46%`, seed `91` `77.82%`, all zero contacts. Small local single-env/drained checks agree directionally: QDR-251 `19.70%`, QDR-282 `61.54%`. Procedural retention seeds `88/90/91` passed at `100%` with zero contacts/timeouts. Fixed-layout seed-88 checks for center_block, doorway, corridor, maze, random_blocks, and layout_mixed all passed at `100%` with zero contacts/timeouts. No-render visual smoke on hard-p1 exposed remaining wall-stall timeouts, so visual behavior is not solved. | This is the missing architectural lever: the actor needed a deployable planner target, not just privileged labels. But the remaining failure is still timeout/stall tails near walls/frontiers, not obstacle contacts. Do not report vector evals without `--drain-initial-episodes` or small-agent confirmation. Next work should improve recovery/unsticking from onboard-target stalls or train a residual around the onboard planner, while preserving the deployable-observation rule. |
| QDR-281 | `region_teacher_deployable_target_student` | Can the QDR-280 privileged `region` teacher improve a deployable MinGRU target student when actor inputs remain real-drone-feasible? Checkpoint sweep at `512` episodes was flat: u200 `26.33%`, u400 `25.59%`, u600 `26.33%`, u800 `27.19%`; all mostly timeouts. | Stronger privileged target labels alone are not enough. | QDR-280 | `privileged_region_frontier_teacher` | Can a stronger privileged frontier-region selector beat the current onboard planner and the old privileged nearest-unexplored teacher, then serve as a high-quality teacher for later deployable distillation? Added `QR_EXPLORE_FRONTIER_TARGET_REGION` / `--frontier-mode region`. It uses privileged map validity, connected unknown regions, local unknown-density gain, clearance score, and path-cost-normalized scoring. Main command: `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-viewpoint-candidate-probe --frontier-mode region --episodes 64 --cases procedural_indoor_hard_p0 procedural_indoor_hard_p1 procedural_indoor_hard_p2 procedural_indoor_hard_p3 --seeds 88 90 91 --max-steps 2048 --max-eval-steps 40000`. Comparison command uses the same settings with `--frontier-mode unexplored`. | Passed as a teacher candidate after one failed scorer. The first large-region scorer was rejected: it chased huge far regions, timed out on hard-p1 (`1.6-6.3%` success, `~92%` coverage), and was too slow. The revised rate selector solved all 12 hard pattern/seed pairs with `100%` success and `0%` obstacle contacts at `2048` steps. It improved `new_cell_rate` over the existing privileged `unexplored` teacher on every pair: p0 `10.55/11.78/13.07` vs `9.40/8.41/11.37`; p1 `2.41/2.42/2.34` vs `1.68/1.69/1.72`; p2 `11.26/11.24/10.09` vs `9.11/9.80/8.36`; p3 `6.94/5.92/7.42` vs `5.31/4.39/5.35`. Coverage remained about `98%`, timeouts/crashes were zero. Runtime is slower than `unexplored`, but practical for diagnostics/label generation. | This is the first clearly stronger privileged exploration teacher. Do use it for labels, diagnostics, and asymmetric/teacher experiments. Do not treat it as deployable actor input: it uses privileged map validity. The next serious build should distill region-teacher targets/actions into deployable observations, and profile/trim the scorer if teacher rollouts become the throughput bottleneck. |
| QDR-279 | `onboard_viewpoint_commitment_probe` | Does hard-p1 lose the strict `2048`-step gate because it churns target/objective choices without new coverage, and can a conservative committed-viewpoint target reduce that waste without adding contacts? Added `onboard_target_switch_rate`, `onboard_objective_switch_rate`, `unproductive_objective_switch_rate`, and opt-in `--explore-onboard-commit-steps` / `--explore-onboard-commit-min-clearance`. Baseline command: `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-viewpoint-candidate-probe --episodes 64 --cases procedural_indoor_hard_p1 --seeds 88 90 91`. Commitment commands: same with `--explore-onboard-commit-steps 48 --explore-onboard-commit-min-clearance 0.35`, `--explore-onboard-commit-steps 96 --explore-onboard-commit-min-clearance 0.25`, and `--explore-onboard-commit-steps 24 --explore-onboard-commit-min-clearance 0.25`; p2 regression used the `24/0.25` setting. | Partially useful, not promotable. Baseline hard-p1 success across seeds 88/90/91 was `87.5%` / `92.3%` / `82.8%`, with `0%` obstacle and unproductive objective switching around `0.14-0.15`. Weak commitment `48/0.35` was safe but barely active (`~0.03` committed-use rate) and only helped seed 88 slightly. Stronger commitment `96/0.25` reduced objective switching/usefully increased commitment (`~0.10` used rate), but made success worse: `85.9%` / `90.6%` / `79.7%`. Short commitment `24/0.25` was the best setting, but still mixed: `87.5%` / `96.9%` / `81.3%`, with `0%` obstacle. Hard-p2 regression stayed clean at `100%` success / `0%` obstacle on all three seeds. | The strict-horizon issue is not solved by hand-coded viewpoint commitment. Some target/objective churn exists and the metrics are worth keeping, but longer commitment trades responsiveness for slower/worse exploration. Do not spend a sweep on commit length. The next lever should be better high-level frontier/region scoring or a learned selector using the candidate diagnostics, not another local commitment heuristic. |
| QDR-278 | `onboard_viewpoint_candidate_probe` | Before training a learned selector, can we prove the PufferLib-native frontier/viewpoint layer actually produces reachable useful candidates in hard procedural layouts? Hypothesis: if hard-p1 stalls are caused by missing candidate geometry, candidate count, viable count, miss rate, clearance, or depth should look broken; if those stats are healthy, the blocker is target commitment/pace/horizon. Commands: `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-viewpoint-candidate-probe --episodes 64 --cases procedural_indoor_hard_p1 procedural_indoor_hard_p2 --seeds 88 90 91`; longer-horizon check: `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-viewpoint-candidate-probe --episodes 64 --cases procedural_indoor_hard_p1 --seeds 88 90 91 --max-steps 4096 --max-eval-steps 40000`. | Passed as a diagnostic. Added native log fields, render overlay, `scripts/quadrotor4_viewpoint_candidate_probe.py`, and `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-viewpoint-candidate-probe`. Default `2048`-step hard-p1 had safe timeouts (`82.8-92.3%` success, `0%` obstacle, `96.2-96.6%` coverage), while hard-p2 solved all seeds (`100%` success, `0%` obstacle, about `98.2%` coverage). Hard-p1 with `4096` steps solved all three seeds (`100%` success, `0%` obstacle, about `98.0%` coverage). Candidate stats were not broken: hard-p1 averaged about `37-40` candidates, `13.7-15.0` viable candidates, `0.55-0.62` used rate, `0.03-0.04` miss rate, about `0.93-1.04m` best clearance, and waypoint depth around `11-12` cells. Hard-p2 had fewer candidates and higher miss rate (`0.49-0.54`) but still solved. | Do not keep testing whether hard-p1 has any candidate signal: it does. The current limit is slow/hesitant conversion of viable viewpoints into timely coverage progress, especially under the `2048`-step horizon. Next build should use the candidate diagnostics to improve commitment, sequencing, or a learned high-level selector; it should not add more one-step local velocity heuristics unless a new probe shows a specific geometric bug. |
| QDR-277 | `research_reassessment_map_graph_exploration_stack` | Are we still working on the right problem after QDR-275/QDR-276, or are we overfitting a local-controller failure that public exploration systems would solve with a different architecture? Reviewed public primary sources and repos: [FUEL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.11561) / [code](https://github.com/HKUST-Aerial-Robotics/FUEL), [TARE](https://publications.ri.cmu.edu/tare-a-hierarchical-framework-for-efficiently-exploring-complex-3d-environments), [Active Neural SLAM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.05155) / [code](https://github.com/devendrachaplot/Neural-SLAM), [FAR Planner](https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.09460) / [code](https://github.com/MichaelFYang/far_planner), [FASTER](https://arxiv.org/abs/2001.04420) / [code](https://github.com/mit-acl/faster), [Learning High-Speed Flight in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.05113) / [code](https://github.com/uzh-rpg/agile_autonomy), [Swift](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-023-06419-4), and [Massive Parallel DRL for Active SLAM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.25834). | Reassessment passed and changes direction. The exploration papers consistently separate persistent map/frontier/graph state, global or long-horizon viewpoint selection, local collision-safe planning, and low-level control. The agile-flight papers use privileged learning and deployable onboard observations for short-horizon flight through clutter, but they do not ask a reactive local-ray PPO policy to invent full exploration. QDR-275/QDR-276 now look like symptoms of missing spatial representation/viewpoint selection, not a scalar MPC weight problem. | Stop spending mainline time on per-step local velocity MPC and small ray-safety heuristics. Keep QDR-270/QDR-275 hard-state probes as diagnostics, but the next engineering build should be a research-aligned exploration stack inside PufferLib: persistent deployable occupancy/frontier state, frontier/viewpoint candidates or a small topological graph, explicit candidate diagnostics, and then a learned high-level selector or privileged-label student. Fast flight remains a separate Swift-lite track. |
| QDR-276 | `onboard_stall_committed_progress_mpc_probe` | Does the QDR-275 local-search failure come from myopic single-step replanning and excessive safety preference? Hypothesis: committing to a selected local action for several steps and weighting coverage progress more heavily should reduce dithering from exact stall states without causing obstacle contacts. Added `--mpc-commit-steps` and explicit MPC scoring weights to `scripts/quadrotor4_onboard_hardstate_probe.py`. GPU command: `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-onboard-hardstate-probe --seed 88 --hard-states 16 --collect-steps 20000 --stall-steps 90 --replay-horizon 256 --mpc-rollout-steps 4 --mpc-commit-steps 8 --mpc-final-coverage-weight 500 --mpc-best-coverage-weight 200 --mpc-clearance-weight 0.05 --mpc-ray-dirs 4 --mode procedural_indoor_hard_p1 --total-agents 64 --num-buffers 1 --num-threads 8`. | Failed. Same 16-state capture signature as QDR-275: coverage `0.4384`, min ray `0.3380m`, since-progress `108.4`, action speed `0.1545`. Baseline replay at horizon `256`: mean coverage `0.5084`, gain `0.06994`, no crashes. Committed/progress-heavy MPC: `0/16` success, `4/16` crashes, mean coverage `0.3927`, mean gain `-0.04574`, best gain `0.04167`, min clearance `0.2709m`. | Do not fix QDR-275 by simply committing longer or rewarding progress harder. In the actual wall-stall states, that trades slow safe progress for obstacle contacts and worse coverage. The next lever needs better state understanding/geometry, not more aggressive local velocity macro-actions. |
| QDR-275 | `onboard_stall_hardstate_mpc_probe` | Can a stronger local trajectory-search controller recover the actual near-wall/no-progress states that remain after QDR-270, or is the remaining stall not fixable at the local-control layer? Hypothesis: if short-horizon candidate action replay improves coverage/success from exact wall-stall snapshots versus the baseline onboard target controller, then the next engineering build should be a learned recovery/residual controller trained from those states. Command: `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-onboard-hardstate-probe --seed 88 --hard-states 16 --collect-steps 20000 --stall-steps 90 --replay-horizon 512 --mpc-rollout-steps 12 --mode procedural_indoor_hard_p1 --total-agents 64 --num-buffers 1 --num-threads 8`. | Failed as a controller, passed as a hard-state feedback loop. Added `scripts/quadrotor4_onboard_hardstate_probe.py` and `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-onboard-hardstate-probe`. Remote GPU backend had to be rebuilt with `pybind11`, `CC=gcc CXX=g++`, and `CUDA_HOME=/usr/local/cuda-13.2`; Torch CUDA works despite an NVML warning. Tiny remote smoke passed. Full reserved GPU command captured 16 real stall states by step `828`: mean coverage `0.4384`, min ray `0.3380m`, since-progress `108.4`, target exists, action speed `0.1545`. Baseline replay at horizon `512` gained `0.1577` coverage, no crashes/successes; full MPC with rollout `12` was stopped as impractically slow. Feasible GPU variant at horizon `256`, rollout `4`: baseline gain `0.06994`, MPC gain `0.07161`, no crashes/successes, MPC clearance `0.3392m` vs baseline `0.3287m`. Local CPU probes had the same signature: short MPC can improve selected traces, but longer replay becomes conservative. | Exact stall replay is now useful, but naive per-step MPC is not a meaningful recovery expert. It mostly trades progress for clearance and is too slow at the full setting. Next test should add commitment/progress-weighted macro-actions (QDR-276) or train a recovery residual from these snapshots; do not keep trying per-step ray-safe MPC as the local controller. |
| QDR-260 | `indoor_explore_large_far_map_new_inputs` | Does hard-p1 fail because the deployable explored/frontier map is too local for a 12m room? Hypothesis: the old `11x11` far map covers only a local bubble in the hard slalom, so the recurrent policy can patrol already-seen space without enough map context. Expand the deployable far map to `21x21`, initialize from QDR-251 with input resizing, and train only new encoder columns so old behavior is protected. | Failed in this form. Code/build passed; obs size changed from `416` to `1056`. GPU train correctly froze everything except encoder columns `416:1056`. Deterministic eval: u200 hard-p1 `1.66%` success / `98.34%` timeout, hard-p2 `86.74%` success but `13.26%` obstacle, aggregate hard `41.28%`. u400 hard-p1 quick eval only `4.68%` success / `95.32%` timeout. Run stopped. | More local map area is not enough when bolted onto the old target module with simple new-column distillation. It helps some p2 coverage but worsens the p1 loop and adds contacts. Next step should change the exploration abstraction: structured/topological frontier memory, a map-planner action target, or a resettable state/action objective that explicitly trains “leave the known loop,” not another passive map input. |
| QDR-261 | `onboard_frontier_target_verifier` | Can we make the next topological/frontier-target direction hard to fool before using it for training? Hypothesis: a deployable onboard frontier target should be verified as a native-env oracle/controller before any distillation; if it cannot solve the hard layouts itself, training a policy to imitate it is wasted. Add `QR_EXPLORE_FRONTIER_TARGET_ONBOARD`, add known-blocked occupancy from range-hit endpoints, add Python mode plumbing, and add `scripts/quadrotor4_onboard_frontier_verify.py` plus `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-onboard-frontier-verify`. | Partially useful, not promotable. Initial onboard target before blocked occupancy was only about `3.13%` success on hard-p1. Known-blocked cells improved the quick p1 oracle to about `17-20%` success / `64%` reachable coverage / `0%` obstacle. The verifier default now uses `--explore-reachable-coverage`; without it, p2 was a false failure because even privileged target modes stalled around `40%` full-layout coverage. With reachable coverage, privileged `nearest` solves p1/p2, onboard p2 reaches about `56%` success / `88%` coverage / `0%` obstacle, but onboard p1 still fails at about `17%` success / `83%` timeout. Tried and rejected: larger unknown-cluster gain scoring (p1 collapsed to `0%`), nearest-safe frontier (`0%` p1), path lookahead (`10%` p1), speed/timeout/safety loosened probes, and blocked-target clear-ray fallback. | Keep the verifier, reachable-coverage default, and blocked-cell layer, but do not train against onboard mode yet. The missing piece is not simply occupancy bookkeeping, timeout length, speed, nearest-frontier selection, unknown-cluster scoring, or clear-ray fallback. The p1 blocker is a stronger onboard spatial/topological memory problem. Next build should add planner-state diagnostics or a more explicit graph/frontier abstraction, then require this verifier to pass before policy training. |
| QDR-262 | `onboard_stale_frontier_manager_probe` | Can a minimal topological frontier manager improve deployable onboard exploration by suppressing stale frontier cells after no coverage progress? Research basis: classic frontier exploration treats frontiers as regions, navigates to accessible unvisited frontiers, and marks inaccessible/non-progressing frontiers so the robot can choose another target. Implement this without privileged map input: persist the current onboard unknown target cell, track coverage progress, mark it stale after `96` env steps without enough new cells, skip stale unknown cells in later target selection, and add `scripts/quadrotor4_planner_probe.py` plus `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-planner-probe` for text traces/CSV. | Improved but not solved. Quick seed-88 p1/p2 verifier after stale suppression: p1 `27.69%` success / `70.04%` coverage / `0%` obstacle; p2 `100%` success / `98.17%` coverage / `0%` obstacle. Three-seed p1/p2 check: p1 seed `88` `29.00%`, seed `90` `33.98%`, seed `91` `24.51%`, all `0%` obstacle; p2 seeds `88/90/91` all `100%`. Planner probe on one hard-p1 episode shows fast early coverage then a stall around `0.626` coverage with target distance back near a local cell, confirming the remaining p1 failure is still local loop/stale-frontier behavior, not low-level flight. Tuning stale ticks: `96` was better than `48`, `192`, or `384`; requiring `6` new cells to reset progress was worse than `2`. | Keep QDR-262 as the current onboard-target build. It validates stale-frontier suppression as a useful mechanism, especially for p2, but p1 remains below the strict verifier threshold. Next step: expose/debug actual selected grid/frontier IDs or move from per-cell stale suppression to clustered frontier regions with region-level stale status and last-seen/last-progress accounting. Do not train a policy against onboard mode until p1 passes the verifier. |
| QDR-263 | `planner_state_debug_and_validator` | Can we make onboard planner correctness visible and testable before training against it? Hypothesis: the next build needs a fast local pass/fail loop for planner state, not another GPU run. Add native planner metrics (`frontier_count`, `stale_frontier_count`, `stale_marks`, `onboard_target_exists`, `onboard_target_age`, `blocked_cells`), draw explored/frontier/stale/blocked/current/target cells in visual eval, and add `scripts/quadrotor4_planner_state_verify.py` plus `scripts/quadrotor4.sh explore-v0-planner-state-verify`. | Passed as tooling. CPU build passed. Visual overlay smoke opened cleanly. Default `explore-v0-planner-state-verify` passed locally: hard-p1 seed 88 oracle `27.69%` success / `70.02%` coverage / `0%` obstacle, hard-p2 `100%` / `98.17%` / `0%`; stall probes on p1/p2 both produced `stale_marks=1`. Sanity rates were clean in this run: `absent_under_goal_rate=0`, `blocked_target_rate=0`. | This does not solve hard-p1, but it gives the missing feedback loop. Before GPU training or distillation against onboard mode, run the planner-state verifier and inspect visual overlays when it fails. The current evidence says hard-p1 is not failing because the target is absent or directly through a wall in the sampled run; it is failing because the per-cell stale planner still lacks region-level memory/progress accounting. |
| QDR-264 | `frontier_cluster_stale_ablation` | Does region-level stale suppression fix the near-wall local frontier flip? Hypothesis from visual log: after no coverage progress, marking only one unknown cell stale leaves neighboring cells in the same local frontier pocket, so the planner picks another tiny target and stays near the wall. Tried three bounded variants before any GPU training: cluster-scored frontier targeting, cluster-stale while keeping per-cell target selection, and earlier stale timeout (`64` ticks). | Failed; reverted active behavior to the QDR-263/QDR-262 per-cell stale default. Cluster-scored targeting was destructive: hard-p1 collapsed to `0%` success and p2 regressed badly. Per-cell target plus cluster/local stale at `96` ticks preserved p2 but did not improve p1 across seeds (`26.47%`, `21.88%`, `36.84%`). Earlier `64`-tick stale was clearly worse on p1 (`9.38%`, `9.38%`, `23.53%`). Final active build keeps the new cluster diagnostics only; 3-seed verifier passed with hard-p2 `100%` and hard-p1 still unsolved at `22.86%`, `36.36%`, `40.54%` success on seeds `88/90/91`, all with `0%` obstacle contacts. | The near-wall stall is not solved by simply suppressing a wider local frontier after the fact. The next real build should model frontier regions as persistent planner objects with visited/stale/inaccessible state and explicit target commitment, or add a small graph/search layer over frontier clusters. Do not repeat one-shot cluster stale marking or shorter stale timeout as standalone fixes. |
| QDR-265 | `onboard_frontier_region_commitment` | Can persistent frontier-region commitment fix the hard-p1 local target churn without the destructive QDR-264 cluster scoring? Hypothesis: the planner keeps retargeting tiny neighboring frontier cells near walls and resets progress accounting; commit to a connected frontier region while it is making progress, avoid resetting progress on intra-region target changes, and stale the committed region only after the no-progress window. | Passed as a planner-level breakthrough, not yet a learned policy. Added `explore_onboard_region_anchor_idx`, connected-region target selection around the committed anchor, stale marking for the committed frontier region, and changed progress accounting so intra-region target churn does not reset the stale timer. CPU build passed. Three-seed p1/p2 verifier with `128` episodes: hard-p1 seed `88` `100%`, seed `90` `99.22%`, seed `91` `99.22%`; hard-p2 seeds `88/90/91` all `100%`; all `0%` obstacle contacts. Broader hard-family gate over p0/p1/p2/p3, seeds `88/90/91`, `64` episodes each: all `100%`, all `0%` obstacle contacts. No-render visual smoke with the wrapper default adjusted to `--restart-on-stall-steps 3000`: `3/3` success, `98.01%` coverage, `0%` obstacle/timeout. | This was the missing piece for the onboard planner: target commitment and stale accounting had to happen at the frontier-region level, not at the most recent tiny cell target. The visual behavior can still pause near walls for long stretches before recovering, so do not call the final explorer solved. Next step is to train/distill a deployable policy or high-level target model against this now-valid planner and keep the planner-state verifier plus visual smoke as regression gates. |
| QDR-266 | `region_commitment_stale_pacing` | Can the QDR-265 committed-region planner use a shorter no-progress stale window to reduce visible near-wall waiting without regressing hard-family success? Hypothesis: QDR-264's `64`-tick stale window failed because target churn kept changing local cells; after QDR-265 region commitment, shorter stale should act on real frontier regions and may reduce sticky visual pauses. | Failed; reverted to `96` ticks. The `64`-tick build passed a loose p1/p2 gate but regressed hard-p1 from QDR-265's `99-100%` to `93.75%`/`96.95%`/`93.75%`. Visual seed `88` got worse: one run lost target existence around `81%` coverage and sat still until the visual stall restart. | Shorter stale is still not the right lever. Even with region commitment, premature stale marking can exhaust local targets and create no-target stalls. Keep `96` ticks. |
| QDR-267 | `onboard_clearance_aware_target` | Can the QDR-265 planner emit a safer local target when the committed frontier waypoint creates a low-clearance cone near a wall? Hypothesis: QDR-265 solves long-horizon region selection but still outputs very short wall-adjacent targets; a deployable ray-based local fallback to the clearest cone should reduce visible target sticking without changing planner memory. | Failed; reverted. The first implementation accidentally patched the privileged unexplored path, not onboard, and had no visual effect. After moving it to onboard, p1/p2 verifier still passed, but no-render visual regressed: `1/2` counted success with one timeout/restart and a long low-coverage stall. | Do not add a free clear-ray escape target as a blanket local fallback. It can make the planner abandon frontier progress and drift into a worse timeout. The next attempt should stay within frontier-region target selection. |
| QDR-268 | `onboard_current_cell_target_penalty` | Can the QDR-265 committed-region planner reduce tiny adjacent wall targets by not over-prefering the current frontier cell when other cells in the same committed region are available? Hypothesis: the region planner's score favors the current cell by depth, so it often outputs an adjacent unknown target; a small current-cell penalty should choose a nearby reachable frontier/viewpoint instead without inventing a clear-ray escape behavior. | Partial improvement; active. Added a `0.35` score penalty for selecting the current cell as the frontier target, in both committed-region and new-region selection. Build passed. No-render visual seed `88`, `3` episodes: `3/3` success, `98.01%` coverage, `0%` obstacle/timeout, and far fewer repeated tiny-target near-wall logs than QDR-265, though one episode still had a long no-progress stretch. P1/P2 verifier with `128` episodes kept hard-p1 at `100%`/`99.22%`/`99.22%` and p2 at `100%`, all `0%` obstacle. Broader hard-family gate over p0/p1/p2/p3 seeds `88/90/91`, `64` episodes each: all `100%`, all `0%` obstacle contacts. A stronger `0.75` penalty produced the same visual trace, so kept the conservative `0.35`. | Keep as a small planner-target hygiene improvement, not a full fix. It reduces the adjacent-current-cell bias without damaging success, but does not eliminate wall-adjacent stalls. The remaining issue is local viewpoint/control quality near walls, not topological region choice alone. |
| QDR-269 | `slide_safety_cone_alignment` | Is the remaining visible near-wall sticking caused by slide safety being more conservative than its configured cone? Hypothesis: `_slide_safety_actions` uses all rays with positive forward dot product, effectively a `180deg` forward half-plane, while the configured safety cone and diagnostics use `±safety_cone_deg`. Restrict slide projection to the same cone so along-wall targets are not slowed by side rays outside the safety cone. | Failed/no useful effect; reverted. Build and visual smoke ran, but seed-88 visual trace was essentially unchanged from QDR-268. P1/P2 verifier still passed, but hard-p1 max-progress-stall did not improve and was noisier. | The sticky case is not caused by rays outside the configured cone. The slowdowns are from genuinely close rays inside the movement cone plus frontier viewpoint geometry. Do not repeat this as the next fix. |
| QDR-270 | `onboard_local_viewpoint_target` | Can the QDR-268 planner reduce near-wall stalls by backing up to a nearby reachable explored viewpoint when the committed frontier target is the current cell plus an adjacent unknown sliver? Hypothesis: region commitment solved the topological target choice, but the emitted local target can still be a tiny wall-adjacent unknown. Pick a short viewpoint endpoint in the same onboard frontier context, using only the deployable explored/blocked grid plus current lidar cone scoring, then keep the existing path-following target emission. | Partial improvement; active. Added a local viewpoint selector for current-cell frontier slivers, restricted to reachable explored cells with known-clear grid sightline to the same unknown target. Also scored candidate first steps by current lidar cone clearance. CPU build passed. No-render visual seed `88`, `3` episodes: `3/3` success, `98.01%` coverage, `0%` obstacle/timeout, `0.8795` new-cell rate. The tiny `0.021m` adjacent-target symptom disappeared in this smoke, but long near-wall progress stalls still appeared. P1/P2 verifier with `128` episodes passed: hard-p1 `100%`/`99.22%`/`100%`, hard-p2 `100%` all seeds, `0%` obstacle. Hard-family p0/p1/p2/p3 gate with `64` episodes across seeds `88/90/91` passed at `100%` success and `0%` obstacle. | Keep QDR-270 as target hygiene, not a solved local controller. It removes the worst tiny-current-cell target emission without hurting the planner ceiling, but the remaining stalls persist because some states have no better reachable/sighted viewpoint in the known local graph. Next real lever is richer local motion/viewpoint planning or policy transfer, not another free escape heuristic. |
| QDR-271 | `onboard_viewpoint_region_planner` | Can we replace one-cell frontier target choice with a real frontier-region plus viewpoint planner? Hypothesis: the planner should compare connected frontier regions by reachable viewpoint quality, expected visible unknown gain, current lidar-cone clearance, and path cost, then emit the first step toward the chosen viewpoint. This is closer to FUEL/Active-Neural-SLAM style hierarchy while still using only deployable onboard grid/ray state. | Failed; reverted active code to QDR-270. First build scored each frontier region by local viewpoint quality and visible unknown gain. Visual seed `88` still succeeded `3/3`, but new-cell rate fell to `0.7412` and wall-stall logs increased. Adding a weighted explored-cell path graph removed stall logs in one visual smoke but regressed p1: hard-p1 `128` episodes gave `96.88%`/`97.66%`/`94.53%` success with timeouts and max-progress-stall above `2200`. A weaker path-cost version still failed the strong p1 gate: `96.88%`/`97.66%`/`93.75%` at `128` episodes. The final active code was cleaned back to QDR-270 behavior and reconfirmed with seed-88 no-render visual `3/3`, `98.01%` coverage, `0%` obstacle/timeout, `0.8795` new-cell rate, plus p1 `64`-episode seeds `88/90/91` all `100%`. | Do not repeat global frontier-region viewpoint scoring or weighted-grid path costs as standalone fixes. They make the planner look more structured but trade away robust p1 completion. The blocker is now below frontier choice: local motion/control near walls needs a trajectory/barrier-style controller or learned low-level policy, while QDR-270 remains the safer planner baseline. |
| QDR-272 | `sampled_local_barrier_controller` | Can a deployable short-horizon local controller reduce near-wall stalls without changing the proven QDR-270 frontier planner? Hypothesis: the bad action is produced after the planner target, when the target vector is scaled/projected by a simple ray safety rule. Sample candidate body-frame velocity directions around the desired target direction, score them by target progress, current lidar cone clearance, and safety speed scale, then command the best safe progress-making velocity. | Failed as a promotion; optional diagnostic flag kept for now as `--safety-sample-actions`. First sampler over-preferred safe sideways motion: visual seed `88` stayed `3/3` but new-cell rate fell to `0.7835`. Progress-dominant scoring improved visual to `0.8819` new-cell rate but p1 `64`-episode max-progress-stall worsened (`1029/821/667`) and new-cell rates dropped versus QDR-270. Triggering the sampler only for low desired safety scale improved visual to `0.9102`, but p1 `64` still worsened max-progress-stall (`969/704/845`) and new-cell rate. Lower trigger `0.25` also failed. Active default remains QDR-270; the sampler is not enabled by wrappers. | The right lever is still local control, but naive direction sampling is not enough. It can make individual visual rollouts look smoother while degrading hard-p1 progress statistics. Next controller should optimize a short trajectory/state objective with progress accounting, not just choose a single ray-safe velocity direction. |
| QDR-273 | `stateful_progress_recovery_controller` | Can a stateful short-horizon recovery controller fix the near-wall stalls that QDR-272's stateless sampler could not? Hypothesis: only override QDR-270 when an agent has made no coverage progress for a window while near a wall. During a short recovery mode, keep a per-agent turn side and choose a rollout-style ray-safe command that improves clearance while preserving some target progress, then return to the default slide controller. | Failed as a promotion; optional diagnostic flag kept as `--safety-stateful-recovery`. Default trigger `360` steps was too conservative: visual seed `88` improved only slightly (`0.8837` new-cell rate) and p1 `64` was effectively QDR-270. Earlier trigger `180` improved visual seed `88` to `0.9147` and kept p1 `64` clean, but full p1/p2 `128` had the same max-progress-stall pattern as QDR-270, so it did not solve the real hard episodes. Wider near-wall trigger `0.50` caused a visual coverage-stall restart and worsened p1 max-stall. Active default remains QDR-270. | Stateful recovery is not sufficient when built from a single local ray snapshot. The hard stalls are not just “turn sideways for N steps”; they need a richer state/trajectory objective or learned recovery policy with explicit progress supervision. |
| QDR-274 | `clear_target_progress_speed_floor` | Can we reduce near-wall stalls by allowing more speed when the desired target ray is clear but the wider safety cone is throttling on a nearby side wall? Hypothesis from visual logs: many stalls have `target_ray`/`action_ray` several meters clear, but `cone_ray` around `0.38-0.45m`, which scales speed to `0.08-0.15`. A deployable progress floor should preserve caution but avoid crawling when the direct target path is clear. | Failed as a promotion; optional flags kept for diagnostics. Tested floor scales `0.55` and `0.45`, alone and combined with QDR-273 recovery. Scale `0.55` gave visual seed `88` `0.8909` new-cell rate but worsened p1 seed `90` max-progress-stall (`731` vs QDR-270 `573`). Scale `0.45` worsened visual rate to `0.8326`. Combining with stateful recovery also underperformed (`0.8686` visual rate, p1 seed `90` max-stall `769`). Default regression after leaving flags off: visual seed `88` `3/3`, `98.01%` coverage, `0%` obstacle/timeout, `0.8795` new-cell rate; p1 `64` seeds `88/90/91` all `100%`, matching QDR-270. | The side-wall speed throttle is a symptom, but a simple floor is not the fix. It changes timing and can worsen systematic p1 stalls without causing contacts. Do not promote speed floors without a trajectory-level safety/progress proof. |
| QDR-259 | `indoor_explore_hard_p1p2_frontier_distill` | Can QDR-251 learn the new hard procedural exploration layouts once we train directly on the failure distribution? Diagnostic before launch: on `procedural_indoor_hard_p1`, QDR-251 + slide safety gets `39.17%` student success / `60.83%` timeout locally while the privileged oracle gets `100%` success. Timeout episodes have much larger student/oracle target disagreement (`86deg` mean) than success episodes (`9.9deg` mean), so the blocker is deployed frontier-direction prediction, not low-level flight or an unsolved teacher. Hypothesis: oversampling hard p1/p2, using hard-example weights for target-angle/clearance mistakes, and anchoring old fixed/procedural modes to QDR-251 should improve hard p1 without forgetting the current baseline. | Failed and stopped. The training HUD is misleading here: it reports successful teacher/student stream episodes while deterministic eval collapses. The blocker is deployable memory/target representation, not simply more hard-mode labels or a different vector fallback threshold. |
| QDR-258 | `indoor_explore_harder_procedural_benchmark` | Can we create the next benchmark where QDR-251 plus deployable slide safety no longer trivially solves the task? Add `procedural_indoor_hard` and forced pattern modes `procedural_indoor_hard_p0..p3`; evaluate QDR-251 + `--safety-slide` before training. | Passed as a benchmark build. Added hard procedural obstacle modes in C and script plumbing. A `4.5m`, `95%` coverage smoke was still too easy (`32/32` success). The promoted stress setting is `room-size 6.0`, `explore-goal-fraction 0.98`, `max-steps 4096`. GPU baseline with QDR-251 + slide safety across seeds `88/90/91`: aggregate hard `52.14%`/`53.97%`/`56.47%` success, coverage `86.28%`/`86.90%`/`87.70%`, timeout `44.75%`/`43.17%`/`40.40%`, obstacle `3.11%`/`2.86%`/`3.13%`. Forced patterns: p0 about `60.9%` success, p1 about `44.7%` with `0%` obstacle and `55%` timeouts, p2 about `52.2%` with `4-6%` obstacle and `41-43%` timeout, p3 about `91.8%` with `7-9%` obstacle and no timeouts. | This is the next useful benchmark. QDR-251 + slide no longer trivially solves it. The main blocker is slow/insufficient exploration under occlusion, especially hard p1; p2/p3 add smaller contact tails. Next work should improve memory/frontier target selection or train on hard procedural layouts, not tune low-level safety. |
| QDR-257 | `indoor_explore_teacher_only_slide_action_refine` | Did QDR-255/QDR-256 fail because mixed student rollouts made the action head chase off-distribution states before it had accurately imitated the slide teacher? The strongest learned-action candidate remains QDR-255 u600 (`87.56%` corridor success / `12.44%` obstacle), fetched locally at `experiments/qdr255/checkpoints/qdr255_slide_action_s88_u000600.pt`. This suggests the action head is not the right abstraction yet; use slide as a deployable low-level safety layer or train a dedicated residual/barrier module with explicit no-contact constraints, not plain action imitation. |
| QDR-256 | `indoor_explore_weighted_slide_action_distill` | Can we close the QDR-255 raw-action contact gap by upweighting the rare states where slide safety makes a large correction or clearance is low? | QDR-255 | `indoor_explore_slide_safety_action_distill` | Can we teach the deployable slide-safety behavior into the actor rather than keeping it as a hardcoded wrapper? Corridor eval without external slide wrapper: u200 `73.42%` success / `26.57%` obstacle, u400 `84.74%` / `15.26%`, u600 `87.56%` / `12.44%`, final `86.89%` / `13.11%`; no timeouts. | Plain action MSE learns to move but not enough near-wall safety. | QDR-243 | `indoor_explore_target_asym_ppo_recurrent_fix` | Does target-space asymmetric PPO become stable when the PPO update replays recurrent actor/critic sequences with the same rollout start states and done resets? Corrected oracle proc95 with reachable coverage: `80.90%` success, `19.01%` timeout, `0.09%` obstacle, coverage `77.30%` over all episodes. QDR-224 student under corrected reachable proc95: `68.71%` success, `30.88%` timeout, `0.41%` obstacle, coverage `74.91%`. Old all-valid proc95 QDR-224 was `65.39%` and the oracle was only about `62-65%`, so the denominator was part of the plateau but not the whole problem. | Keep reachable-component coverage as the valid procedural exploration benchmark.
